Dottorato di Ricerca in Matematica


Abstract




XXXII Ciclo

Dottorato in Matematica

The Norm map on the compactified Jacobian,

the Prym stack

and

Spectral data for G-Higgs pairs

RAFFAELE CARBONE

Nome e Cognome del dottorando

Firma

FILIPPO VIVIANI

Docente Guida/Tutor: Prof.

Firma

ANGELO LOPEZ

Coordinatore: Prof.

Firma

Introduction and statement of results

Let
C
be a fixed smooth and projective curve over
\mathbb{C}
. A (
L
-twisted)
Higgs pair
on
C
is a pair
(E,\Phi)
of a vector bundle
E
on
C
and a endomorphism
\Phi:E\rightarrow E\otimes L
with values in a line bundle
L
on
C
. Higgs pairs were introduced first in the case
L=K_{C}
under the name of Higgs bundles by N. Hitchin, in his study about dimensionally reduced self-duality equations of Yang-Mills gauge theory(
[
Hit87b
]
,
[
Hit87a
]
), and then by C. Simpson, in his study of nonabelian Hodge theory (
[
Sim92
]
,
[
Sim94a
]
,
[
Sim94b
]
).
L
-twisted Higgs pairs were introduced by N. Nitsure
[
Nit92
]
.

If
E
has a
G
-structure, where
G
is some complex reductive group, and
\Phi
satisfies some extra conditions depending on
G
, we speak of
G
-Higgs pairs and
G
-Higgs bundles. Hitchin
[
Hit87a
]
, followed by R. Donagi
[
Don93
]
, showed that the moduli space
M_{G}
of semistable
G
-Higgs bundles is endowed with a proper map
H_{G}
to a vector space, whose generic fiber is a complex Lagrangian torus and an abelian variety. Such map is called
G
-
Hitchin fibration
and makes
M_{G}
an algebraically completely integrable system; moreover, the smooth locus of
M_{G}
has the structure of a hyperkähler manifold. More generally, the Hitchin fibration
H_{G}
on the moduli space
M_{G}^{L}
of semistable
G
-Higgs pairs was introduced by N. Nitsure (loc. cit.); when
G=\mathrm{GL}
and
L\otimes K_{C}^{-1}
is effective, E. Markman
[
Mar94
]
and F. Bottacin
[
Bot95
]
proved that
M_{G}^{L}
is endowed with a Poisson structure (depending upon the choice of a section of
L\otimes K_{C}^{-1}
) with respect to which
H_{G}
becomes an algebraically completely integrable system.

T. Hausel and M. Thaddeus
[
HT03
]
related the
G
-Hitchin fibration to mirror symmetry, while A. Kapustin and E. Witten
[
KW07
]
pointed out on physical grounds that Hitchin’s system for a complex reductive Lie group
G
is dual to Hitchin’s system for the Langlands dual group
{}^{L}G
. This was proved in an algebro-geometric setting by R. Donagi and T. Pantev
[
DP12
]
; they also conjectured the
classical limit of the geometric Langlands correspondence
as a canonical equivalence between the derived categories of coherent sheaves over the moduli stacks of
G
-Higgs bundles and
{}^{L}G
-Higgs bundles, which intertwines the action of the classical limit tensorization functors with the action of the classical limit Hecke functors. Recently, M. Groechenig, D. Wyss and P. Ziegler
[
GWZ17
]
proved, using an arithmetic perspective, a conjecture by Hausel-Thaddues stating that the moduli spaces of
\mathrm{SL}
and
\mathrm{PGL}
-Higgs bundles are mirror partners and that appropriately defined Hodge numbers of such spaces agree.

The connection between Hitchin system and geometric Langlands program led to the study of Lagrangian submanifolds of the moduli space of Higgs bundles supporting holomorphic sheaves (A-branes), and their dual objects (B-branes). This study was introduced by A. Kapustin and E. Witten in loc. cit. (see also E. Witten
[
Wit15
]
), followed by L. Schaposhnik and D. Baraglia (
[
BS14
]
,
[
BS16
]
) and L. Branco
[
Bra18
]
.

More recently, M. de Cataldo, T. Hausel and L. Migliorini
[
dCHM12
]
followed by J. Shen and Z. Zhang
[
SZ18
]
and M. de Cataldo, D. Maulik and J. Shen
[
dCMS19
]
studied the “P=W” conjecture, stating that the canonical isomorphism

H^{*}(\mathcal{M}_{B},\mathbb{Q})\simeq H^{*}(\mathcal{M}(r),\mathbb{Q})

induced by canonical diffeomorphism between the moduli space
\mathcal{M}(r)
of rank
r
Higgs bundle and the corresponding character variety
\mathcal{M}_{B}
of rank
r
stable local systems, identifies the weight filtration and the perverse filtration associated with the Hitchin fibration.

The generic fiber of the
\mathrm{GL}
-Hitchin morphism is the Jacobian of a curve associated to the fiber, called the
spectral curve
; when
G=\mathrm{SL},\mathrm{Sp},\mathrm{SO}
, the generic fiber of the
G
-Hitchin morphisms are Prym varieties associated to certain morphisms from the spectral curve. This fact, also known as
spectral correspondence
or
abelianization process
, has been pointed out first in
[
Hit87a
]
, followed by
[
Hit07
]
and
[
Sch13
]
. The duality of Hitchin systems manifests itself in the statement that the dual of the abelian variety for the generic fiber of
\mathcal{H}_{G}
is the abelian variety for the generic fiber of
\mathcal{H}_{{}^{L}G}
. More recently, N. Hitchin and L. Schaposnik introduced in
[
HS14
]
a
non-abelianization
process in order to study Higgs bundles that correspond (by solving the gauge-theoretic Higgs bundle equations) to flat connections on
C
with holonomy in some real Lie groups.

The study of the Hitchin morphism restricted to the fibers whose associated spectral curve is integral played a crucial role in B. C. Ngô’s proof of the fundamental lemma (
[
Ngô06
]
and
[
Ngô10
]
); more generally, the study of the Hitchin morphism restricted to the fibers whose associated spectral curve is reduced was a key ingredient in Chaudard-Laumon’s proof of the weighted fundamental lemma (
[
CL10
]
and
[
CL12
]
).

For
G=\mathrm{GL}
the spectral correspondence has been generalized to non-generic fibers with integral spectral curve by A. Beauville, M. Narasimhan and S. Ramanan in
[
BNR89
]
, and to any fiber by D. Schaub
[
Sch98
]
, followed by P.-H. Chaudouard and G. Laumon
[
CL16
]
and M. A. De Cataldo
[
dC17
]
. The spectral correspondence for non-generic fibers involves a wider moduli space than the Jacobian scheme, namely the
compactified Jacobian
parametrizing torsion-free rank-1 sheaves.

For
G
different from
\mathrm{GL}
, the spectral correspondence for non-generic fibers has been studied by
[
HP12
]
for the case of
\mathrm{SL}
, and by
[
Bra18
]
for the Hitchin map associated to some semisimple real Lie groups.

This thesis is divided in two parts, introduced by a preliminary Chapter about torsion-free rank-1 sheaves and Higgs pairs (Chapter
1
). In the first part (Chapter
2
and
3
), motivated by the spectral correspondence for
G=\mathrm{SL}
, we study the Norm map
\operatorname{Nm}_{\pi}
on the compactified Jacobian associated to a finite, flat morphism
X\xrightarrow{\pi}Y
between projective curves; the Norm map happens to be well-defined only if
Y
is smooth. In such case, we define the Prym stack of
X
over
Y
as the (stacky) fiber
\operatorname{Nm}^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{Y})
. In the case that
X
is reduced with locally planar singularities, we show that the usual Prym scheme is contained in the Prym stack as an open and dense subset. In the second part (Chapter
4
) we study the spectral correspondence for
G
-Higgs pairs, in the case of
G=\mathrm{SL}(r,\mathbb{C})
,
\mathrm{PGL}(r,\mathbb{C})
,
\mathrm{Sp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
,
\mathrm{GSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
,
\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
, over any fiber.

In the future work, we aim to study the spectral data also for other classical groups such as
\mathrm{SO}(2r,\mathbb{C})
and
\mathrm{SO}(2r+1,\mathbb{C})
. Moreover, we aim to study the geometric properties of the moduli loci arising in the description of the spectral data. Finally, we are interested in characterizing, for various
G
, the spectral data corresponding to semistable pairs, and to consider also such data up to
S
-equivalence of the corresponding pairs; this would allow to describe spectral data for the scheme-theoretic version of the Hitchin morphism.

Acknowledgements
. First, I would like to thank my advisor, Filippo Viviani, for suggesting the problem and for his continuous guidance and proofreading. I would also like to thank Edoardo Sernesi for helpful comments and discussions, together with Eduardo Esteves and Margarida Melo. Finally, I thank my collegues Fabrizio Anella and Daniele Di Tullio for their help and patient listening during these years.

0.1.
Preliminaries

In Chapter
1
we first introduce generalized line bundles and torsion-free rank-1 sheaves, giving the following general definitions.

Definition 0.1.1
.

Let
X
be a Noetherian scheme of pure dimension 1. A coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
X
is said:

(1)

torsion-free
if the support of
\mathcal{F}
has dimension 1 and the maximal subsheaf
T(\mathcal{F})\subset\mathcal{F}
of dimension 0 is equal to 0;

(2)

rank-1
if for any generic point
\xi\in X
, the length of
\mathcal{F}_{\xi}
as an
\mathcal{O}_{X,\xi}
-module is equal to the length of
\mathcal{O}_{X,\xi}
as a module over itself.

Definition 0.1.2
.

Let
X
be a Noetherian scheme of pure dimension 1. A
generalized line bundle
is a torsion-free sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
X
such that for any generic point
\xi\in X
,the stalk
\mathcal{F}_{\xi}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{X,\xi}
as an
\mathcal{O}_{X,\xi}
-module.

In the case of
X
projective over a field, generalized line bundles and torsion-free rank-1 sheaves are both particular cases of torsion-free sheaves with polarized rank 1 with respect to any fixed polarization.

Definition 0.1.3
.

Let
X
be a projective scheme of pure dimension 1 over a base field
k
and let
H
be a polarization of degree
\deg H=\delta
. Let
\mathcal{F}
be a torsion-free sheaf on
X
. The
polarized rank
of
\mathcal{F}
is the rational number
r_{H}(\mathcal{F})
determined by the Hilbert polinomial of
\mathcal{F}
with respect to
H
:

P(\mathcal{F},n,H):=\chi(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(nH))=\delta r_{H}(\mathcal{F})n+\chi(X,\mathcal{F}).

The polarized rank of a torsion-free sheaf is related with its rank at the generic points.

Theorem 0.1.4
.

Let
X
be a projective scheme of pure dimension 1 over a field, with irreducible components
X_{1},\dots,X_{s}
and let
\mathcal{F}
be a torsion-free sheaf on
X
. For each
i
, let
\xi_{i}
be the generic point of
X_{i}
and let
\operatorname{rk}_{X_{i}}(\mathcal{F}):=\ell_{\mathcal{O}_{X,\xi_{i}}}(\mathcal{F}_{\xi_{i}})/\ell_{\mathcal{O}_{X,\xi_{i}}}(\mathcal{O}_{X,\xi_{i}})
be the rank of
\mathcal{F}
at
X_{i}
. Let
H
be a polarization on
X
. Then, the following formula for
r_{H}(\mathcal{F})
holds:

r_{H}(\mathcal{F})=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{s}\operatorname{rk}_{X_{i}}(\mathcal{F})\deg H_{|X_{i}}}{\sum_{i=1}^{s}\deg H_{|X_{i}}}.

In particular, if
\mathcal{F}
is a torsion-free rank-1 sheaf, then
r_{H}(\mathcal{F})=1
for any polarization
H
on
X
.

We consider the following moduli spaces for torsion-free sheaves.

Definition 0.1.5
.

Let
X
be a projective scheme of pure dimension 1 over a field
k
and let
d\in\mathbb{Z}
be an integer number.

(1)

The
Jacobian scheme
of degree
d
on
X
is the algebraic scheme
J^{d}(X)
representing the sheafification of the functor that associates to any
k
-scheme
T
the set of isomorphism classes of line bundles of degree
d
on
X\times_{k}T
. The union of the Jacobians of all degrees is denoted as
J(X)
.

(2)

The
generalized Jacobian stack
of degree
d
on
X
is the algebraic stack
\operatorname{\textit{G}\mathbb{J}}(X,d)
such that, for any
k
-scheme
T
,
\operatorname{\textit{G}\mathbb{J}}(X,d)(T)
is the groupoid of
T
-flat coherent sheaves on
X\times_{k}T
whose fibers over
T
are generalized line bundles of degree
d
on
X\simeq X\times_{k}\{t\}
.

(3)

The
compactified Jacobian stack
of degree
d
on
X
is the algebraic stack
\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d)
such that for any
k
-scheme
T
,
\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d)(T)
is the groupoid of
T
-flat coherent sheaves on
X\times_{k}T
whose fibers over
T
are torsion-free sheaves of rank 1 and degree
d
on
X\simeq X\times_{k}\{t\}
.

(4)

Let
H
be a polarization on
X
The
Simpson Jacobian stack
of degree
d
on
X
is the algebraic stack
\overline{\mathbb{J}}_{tf}(X,H,d)
such that for any
k
-scheme
T
,
\overline{\mathbb{J}}_{tf}(X,H,d)(T)
is the groupoid of
T
-flat coherent sheaves on
X\times_{k}T
whose fibers over
T
are torsion-free sheaves of polarized rank 1 and polarized degree
d
on
X\simeq X\times_{k}\{t\}
with respect to
H
.

The locus of semi-stable object can be defined in any of such moduli spaces, depending on the choiche of the polarization
H
. Then, we have the following good moduli spaces.

(5)

The
generalized Jacobian scheme

\operatorname{\textit{GJ}}(X,H,d)
representing S-equivalence classes of
H
-semistable generalized line bundles of degree
d
on
X
.

(6)

The
compactified Jacobian scheme

\overline{J}(X,H,d)
representing S-equivalence classes of
H
-semistable torsion-free sheaves of rank 1 and degree
d
on
X
.

(7)

The
Simpson Jacobian

\overline{J}_{tf}(X,H,d)
representing S-equivalence classes of
H
-semistable torsion-free sheaves of polarized rank 1 and polarized degree
d
on
X
.

These moduli spaces satisfy the following chains of inclusion:

J^{d}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1)}}{{\subseteq}}\operatorname{\textit{G}\mathbb{J}}(X,d)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2)}}{{\subseteq}}\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(3)}}{{\subseteq}}\overline{\mathbb{J}}_{tf}(X,H,d),

and:

J^{d}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1^{\prime})}}{{\subseteq}}\operatorname{\textit{GJ}}(X,H,d)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2^{\prime})}}{{\subseteq}}\overline{J}(X,H,d)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(3^{\prime})}}{{\subseteq}}\overline{J}_{tf}(X,H,d).

All the inclusions above are open embeddings, non strict in general; inclusions
(3)
and
(3^{\prime})
are also closed. When
X
satisfies additional conditions, some of them are actually equalities.

•

If
X
is irreducible, inclusion
(3)
(resp.
(3)^{\prime}
) is an equality.

•

If
X
is reduced, inclusion
(2)
(resp.
(2^{\prime})
) is an equality.

•

If
X
is integral and smooth, inclusions
(1)
,
(2)
and
(3)
(resp.
(1^{\prime})
,
(2^{\prime})
and
(3^{\prime})
) are equalities.

The moduli space
\overline{J}_{tf}(X,H,d)
of semistable torsion-free sheaves of polarized rank
1
and degree
d
is projective by the work of C. Simpson (
[
Sim94a
]
and
[
Sim94b
]
); hence, the compactified Jacobian
\overline{J}(X,H,d)
is a projective subscheme and union of connected components.

The compactified Jacobian plays an important role in the spectral correspondence for Higgs pairs. Let
C
be a fixed smooth curve over the field of complex numbers and let
L
be a fixed line bundle on
C
with degree
\ell=\deg L
.
The algebraic stack
\mathcal{M}(r,d)
of all
L
-twisted Higgs pairs
(E,\Phi)
on
C
of rank
r
and degree
d
is endowed with a morphism, called the
Hitchin morphism
, defined as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{r,d}:\mathcal{M}(r,d)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathcal{A}(r)=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}H^{0}(C,L^{i})

\displaystyle(E,\Phi)

\displaystyle\mapsto(a_{1}(E,\Phi),\dots,a_{r}(E,\Phi))

where
L^{i}=L^{\otimes i}
,
a_{i}(E,\Phi):=(-1)^{i}\operatorname{tr}(\wedge^{i}\Phi)
and
\mathcal{A}(r)
is called the
Hitchin base
. Similarly, the good moduli space
M(r,d)
parametrizing S-equivalence classes of semistable
L
-twisted Higgs pairs of rank
r
and degree
d
is endowed with a flat projective morphism, called the
Hitchin fibration
:

\displaystyle H_{r,d}:M(r,d)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathcal{A}(r)=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}H^{0}(C,L^{i})

\displaystyle(E,\Phi)

\displaystyle\mapsto(a_{1}(E,\Phi),\dots,a_{r}(E,\Phi)).

Let
{\underline{a}}\in\mathcal{A}(r)
be any characteristic. The spectral curve
X_{\underline{a}}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\underline{a}}}C
is the projective scheme defined in the total space of
L
,
P=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{O}_{C}\oplus L^{-1})\xrightarrow{p}C
, by the homogeneous equation

x^{r}+p^{*}(a_{1})x^{r-1}y+\dots+p^{*}(a_{r})y^{r}=0

where
x
is the section of
\mathcal{O}_{P}(1)\otimes p^{*}(L)
whose pushforward via
p
corresponds to the constant section
(1,0)
of
L\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}
and
y
is the section of
\mathcal{O}_{P}(1)
whose pushforward via
p
corresponds to the constant section
(1,0)
of
\mathcal{O}_{C}\otimes L^{-1}
. The spectral curve
X_{\underline{a}}
has pure dimension 1 and canonical sheaf
\omega_{X_{\underline{a}}}=\pi_{\underline{a}}^{*}(\omega_{c}\otimes L^{r-1})
. The following spectral correspondence is a classical result.

Proposition 0.1.6
.

(Spectral correspondence)

Let
{\underline{a}}\in\mathcal{A}(r)
be any characteristic and let
X_{\underline{a}}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\underline{a}}}C
be the associated spectral curve. Let
\mathcal{O}_{C}(1)
be an ample line bundle on
C
; let
\mathcal{O}_{X_{\underline{a}}}(1):=\pi_{\underline{a}}^{*}\big(\mathcal{O}_{C}(1)\big)
be the ample line bundle on
X_{\underline{a}}
obtained by pullback and denote with
H
the associated polarization on
X_{\underline{a}}
.

(1)

For any integer
d
, there is an isomorphism of stacks:

\ \mathcal{H}_{r,d}^{-1}({\underline{a}})\xrightarrow[\Pi]{\hskip 8.19447pt{}_{\sim}\hskip 8.19447pt}\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X_{\underline{a}},d^{\prime}),

where
d^{\prime}=d+r(1-g)-\chi(\mathcal{O}_{X_{\underline{a}}})=d+\frac{r(r-1)}{2}\ell
and
g=g(C)
is the genus of
C
. If
\mathcal{M}
is a torsion-free sheaf of rank 1 on
X_{\underline{a}}
, then
\Pi(\mathcal{M}):=(E,\Phi)
where

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle=\pi_{{\underline{a}},*}(\mathcal{M})

\displaystyle\Phi

\displaystyle=\pi_{{\underline{a}},*}(\cdot x):\pi_{{\underline{a}},*}(\mathcal{M})\rightarrow L\otimes\pi_{{\underline{a}},*}(\mathcal{M})\simeq\pi_{{\underline{a}},*}(\pi_{\underline{a}}^{*}L\otimes\mathcal{M}).

(2)

A torsion-free sheaf of rank 1
\mathcal{M}
on
X_{\underline{a}}
is
H
-semistable if and only if the associated Higgs pair
\Pi(\mathcal{M})
is semistable on
C
. Hence, the above correspondence yields an isomorphism of schemes:

H_{r,d}^{-1}({\underline{a}})\simeq\overline{J}(X_{\underline{a}},H,d^{\prime}).

The inverse direction in the spectral correspondence is resumed by the following proposition.

Proposition 0.1.7
.

Let
{\underline{a}}\in\mathcal{A}(r)
be any characteristic and let
X_{\underline{a}}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\underline{a}}}C
be the associated spectral curve. Let
\mathcal{M}\in\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X_{\underline{a}},d^{\prime})
be a torsion-free rank-1 sheaf on
X_{\underline{a}}
corresponding to the Higgs pair
(E,\Phi)\in\mathcal{H}_{r,d}^{-1}({\underline{a}})
on
C
. Then, the following exact sequence holds:

0\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi_{\underline{a}}^{*}(L^{1-r})\rightarrow\pi_{\underline{a}}^{*}E\xrightarrow{\pi_{\underline{a}}^{*}(\Phi)-x}\pi_{\underline{a}}^{*}E\otimes\pi_{\underline{a}}^{*}L\xrightarrow{\ev}\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi_{\underline{a}}^{*}L\rightarrow 0

where
\ev
is induced by the evaluation map
\pi_{\underline{a}}^{*}\pi_{{\underline{a}},*}(\mathcal{M})\rightarrow\mathcal{M}
.

0.2.
Direct image of generalized divisors and Norm map

Let
X\xrightarrow{\pi}Y
be a finite, flat morphism between projective schemes of pure dimension 1 over a field, such that
Y
is smooth. In Chapter
2
we introduce two pairs of important morphisms associated to
\pi
.

Definition 0.2.1
.

(1)

The
direct and inverse image map
between the Hilbert schemes
\operatorname{Hilb}_{X}^{d}
and
\operatorname{Hilb}_{Y}^{d}
parametrizing 0-dimensional subschemes of
X
(resp. of
Y
) with Hilbert polynomial equal to
d\geq 0
are defined on the
T
-valued points as:

\displaystyle\pi_{*}(T):\hskip 20.00003pt\operatorname{Hilb}_{X}^{d}(T)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hilb}_{Y}^{d}(T)

\displaystyle D\subseteq X\times_{k}T

\displaystyle\longmapsto\mathcal{Z}\left(\operatorname{Fitt}_{0}(\pi_{T,*}(\mathcal{O}_{D}))\right)

\displaystyle\pi^{*}(T):\hskip 20.00003pt\operatorname{Hilb}_{Y}^{d}(T)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hilb}_{X}^{d}(T)

\displaystyle D\subseteq Y\times_{k}T

\displaystyle\longmapsto\mathcal{Z}\left(\pi_{T}^{-1}(\mathcal{I}_{D})\cdot\mathcal{O}_{X\times_{k}T}\right)

where
\pi_{T}:X\times_{k}T\rightarrow Y\times_{k}T
is the morphism induced by base change of
\pi
,
\operatorname{Fitt}_{0}
denotes the
0
-th Fitting ideal of a sheaf of modules and
\mathcal{Z}
denotes the closed subscheme defined by a sheaf of ideals.

(2)

The
Norm and the inverse image map
between the compactified Jacobians of any degree
d
on
X
and
Y
are defined on the
T
-valued points as:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Nm}_{\pi}(T):\hskip 20.00003pt\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d)(T)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow J^{d}(Y)(T)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\det\left(\pi_{T,*}(\mathcal{L})\right)\otimes\det\left(\pi_{T,*}\mathcal{O}_{X\times_{k}T}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\pi^{*}(T):\hskip 20.00003ptJ^{d}(Y)(T)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow J^{d}(X)\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d)(T)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\pi_{T}^{*}(\mathcal{N}).

Recall that, for any line bundle
M
of degree
e
on
X
, the
M
-twisted Abel map relates
\operatorname{Hilb}_{X}^{d}
with the generalized Jacobian of degree
-d+e
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{M}:\hskip 20.00003pt\operatorname{Hilb}_{X}^{d}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\operatorname{\textit{G}\mathbb{J}}(X,-d+e)\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,-d+e)

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle\longmapsto\mathcal{I}_{D}\otimes M.

Proposition 0.2.2
.

Let
X\xrightarrow{\pi}Y
be a finite, flat morphism between projective schemes of pure dimension 1 over a field, such that
Y
is smooth. The direct image between Hilbert schemes and the Norm map between compactified Jacobians are related for any
d\geq 0
by the commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Hilb}_{X}^{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{{}^{\ell}Hilb}_{Y}^{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{\textit{G}\mathbb{J}}(X,-d+e)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,-d+e)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J^{-d+e}(Y).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}_{\operatorname{Nm}_{\pi}(M)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Nm}_{\pi}}

Similarly, the inverse image between Hilbert schemes and the inverse image between compactified Jacobians are related for any
d\geq 0
by the commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{{}^{\ell}Hilb}_{Y}^{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{{}^{\ell}Hilb}_{X}^{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J(Y,-d+e)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J(X,-d+e).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}_{N}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}_{\pi^{*}(N)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{*}}

The Norm and the inverse image map between compactified Jacobians satisfy some expected properties.

Proposition 0.2.3
.

Let
X\xrightarrow{\pi}Y
be a finite, flat morphism between projective schemes of pure dimension 1 over a field, such that
Y
is smooth. Let
\operatorname{Nm}_{\pi}
and
\pi^{*}
be the Norm and inverse image map between the compactified Jacobans of
X
and
Y
and let
T
be any
k
-scheme.

(1)

Let
\mathcal{L},\mathcal{M}\in\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X)(T)
such that
\mathcal{M}
is a
T
-flat family of line bundles. Then,
\operatorname{Nm}_{\pi}(\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{M})\simeq\operatorname{Nm}_{\pi}(\mathcal{L})\otimes\operatorname{Nm}_{\pi}(\mathcal{M})
.

(2)

Let
\mathcal{N},\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\in J(Y)(T)
. Then,
\pi^{*}(\mathcal{N}\otimes\mathcal{N}^{\prime})\simeq\pi^{*}(\mathcal{N})\otimes\pi^{*}(\mathcal{N}^{\prime})
.

(3)

Let
\mathcal{N}\in J(Y)(T)
. Then,
\pi^{*}(\mathcal{N})
is a
T
-flat family of line bundles over
X
and
\operatorname{Nm}_{\pi}(\pi^{*}(\mathcal{N}))\simeq\mathcal{N}^{\otimes n}
.

The fibers of the direct image and of the Norm map are studied in Chapter
3
, in the case that
X
is reduced with locally planar singularities.

Proposition 0.2.4
.

Let
X\xrightarrow{\pi}Y
be a finite, flat morphism between projective schemes of pure dimension 1 over a field, such that
Y
is smooth and
X
is reduced with locally planar singularities.

(1)

Let
E\in\operatorname{Hilb}_{Y}
be an effective divisor of degree
d
on
Y
and let
\pi_{*}^{-1}(E)
be the corresponding fiber in
\operatorname{Hilb}_{X}
. Then, the locus of Cartier divisors in
\pi_{*}^{-1}(E)
is an open and non-empty dense subset of
\pi_{*}^{-1}(E)
.

(2)

Let
\mathcal{N}\in J(Y)
be any line bundle on
Y
. Then, the fiber
\operatorname{Nm}_{\pi}^{-1}(\mathcal{N})
is non-empty and contains
\operatorname{Nm}_{\pi}^{-1}(\mathcal{N})\cap J(Y)
as an open and dense subset.

In particular, the fiber of
\operatorname{Nm}_{\pi}
over
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
is called the
Prym stack
of
X
associated to
\pi
. When
Y
is smooth and
X
is reduced with locally planar singularities, the Prym variety contained in the Prym stack of
X
associated to
\pi
as an open and dense subset.

0.3.
Spectral data for
G
-Higgs pairs

In Chapter
4
we study the spectral correspondence for
G
-Higgs pairs, where
G=\mathrm{SL}(r,\mathbb{C})
,
\mathrm{PGL}(r,\mathbb{C})
,
\mathrm{Sp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
,
\mathrm{GSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
,
\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
. Throughout this chapter,
C
denotes a fixed smooth curve over the field of complex numbers and
L
a fixed line bundle on
C
with degree
\ell=\deg L
.

Definition 0.3.1
.

Let
G
be a complex reductive Lie group. A
(
L
-twisted)
G
-Higgs pair
is a pair
(P,\phi)
where
P
is a principal
G
-bundle over
C
and the
G
-Higgs field
\phi
is a section of
H^{0}(\ad(P)\otimes L)
.

Let
\delta\in\pi_{1}(G)
and let
p_{1},\dots,p_{k}
be a homogeneous basis for the algebra of invariant polynomials of
the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
of
G
, such that
p_{i}
has degree
d_{i}
. Then, the algebraic moduli stack
\mathcal{M}_{G}(\delta)
of all
L
-twisted
G
-Higgs pairs
(P,\phi)
such that
P
has topological type
\delta
is endowed with a morphism, called the
G
-
Hitchin morphism
, defined as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{G,\delta}^{p_{1},\dots,p_{k}}=\mathcal{H}_{G,\delta}:\mathcal{M}_{G}(\delta)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{G}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}H^{0}(C,L^{d_{i}})

\displaystyle(P,\phi)

\displaystyle\mapsto(p_{1}(\phi),\dots,p_{k}(\phi)).

If
G\xhookrightarrow{\rho}\mathrm{GL}(r,\mathbb{C})
, any
G
-Higgs pair
(P,\phi)
gives rise via the associated bundle construction to a classical Higgs pair
(E,\Phi)
, where
E=P\times_{\rho}\mathbb{C}^{r}
and
\Phi
is the image
\phi
with respect to the morphism
\ad(P)\rightarrow\operatorname{End}(E)
induced by the embedding
\rho
; such
E
has rank
r
and degree
d
corresponding to
\delta
under the map
\pi_{1}(G)\xrightarrow{\pi_{1}(\rho)}\pi_{1}(\mathrm{GL}(r,\mathbb{C}))
induced by
\rho
. In this case, we denote as

\mathcal{M}_{G}(r,d):=\coprod_{\pi_{1}(\rho)(\delta)=d}\mathcal{M}_{G}(\delta)

the moduli space of
G
-Higgs pairs whose associated vector bundle is of rank
r
and degree
d
; the
G
-Hitchin morphism on
\mathcal{M}_{G}(r,d)
is denoted as
\mathcal{H}_{G,r,d}
.

0.3.1.
\boldsymbol{G=\mathrm{SL}(r,\mathbb{C})}

In this case, the datum
(P,\phi)
of a
\mathrm{SL}(r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pair on
C
corresponds univocally, via the associated bundle construction, to the datum of
(E,\Phi,\lambda)
, where
(E,\Phi)
is a Higgs pair of rank
r
on
C
with trace zero, and
\lambda
is a trivialization of
\det E
. The isomorphism
\det E\simeq\mathcal{O}_{C}
implies in particular that
E
has degree equal to 0.

A basis for the invariant polynomials of
\mathfrak{sl}(r,\mathbb{C})
is given by
\{p_{2},\dots,p_{r}\}
where
p_{i}(P,\phi):=(-)^{i}\operatorname{tr}(\wedge^{i}\phi)
. The
\mathrm{SL}
-Hitchin morphism of rank
r
can be defined as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{SL},r}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{SL}}(r):=\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{SL}}(r,0)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{SL}}(r)=\bigoplus_{i=2}^{r}H^{0}(C,L^{i})

\displaystyle(P,\phi)

\displaystyle\longmapsto(p_{2}(P,\phi),\dots,p_{r}(P,\phi)).

Proposition 0.3.2
.

Let
{\underline{a}}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{SL}}(r)
be any characteristic, let
X=X_{\underline{a}}\xrightarrow{\pi}C
be the associated spectral curve, and denote

B:=\det(\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X})^{-1}
. The fiber
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{SL},r}^{-1}({\underline{a}})
of the
\mathrm{SL}
-Hitchin morphism is isomorphic, via the spectral correspondence, to the fiber
\operatorname{Nm}_{\pi}^{-1}(B)
of the Norm map from
\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d^{\prime})
to
J(Y,d^{\prime})
induced by
\pi
for
d^{\prime}=\frac{r(r-1)}{2}\ell
. If the spectral curve
X_{\underline{a}}
is reduced, then

\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{SL},r}^{-1}(a)\simeq\overline{\Pr}(X,C).

0.3.2.
\boldsymbol{G=\mathrm{PGL}(r,\mathbb{C})}

Any
\mathrm{PGL}(r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pair admits a lifting
(\tilde{P},\tilde{\phi})
to a
\mathrm{GL}(r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pair, corresponding to a Higgs pair
(E,\Phi)
via the associated bundle construction. Then, the datum of
(P,\phi)
corresponds uniquely to the datum of the equivalence class
[(E,\Phi)]
of Higgs pairs on
C
with trace zero, under the equivalence relation
\sim_{J(C)}
defined by:

(E,\Phi)\sim_{J(C)}(E\otimes N,\Phi\otimes 1_{N})\hskip 20.00003pt\textrm{for any }N\in J(C).

Denote with
\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{tr}=0}(r)
the closed substack of
\mathcal{M}(r)=\coprod_{d\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{M}(r,d)
given by Higgs pairs of rank
r
with trace zero. Then,
[(E,\Phi)]
is the orbit of
(E,\Phi)
under the action of
J(C)
on
\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{tr}=0}(r)
defined by:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{tr}=0}(r)\times J(C)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{tr}=0}(r)

\displaystyle((E,\Phi),N)

\displaystyle\longmapsto(E\otimes N,\Phi\otimes 1_{N})

Definition 0.3.3
.

Let
(P,\phi)
be a
\mathrm{PGL}
-Higgs pair and let
(E,\Phi)
be a Higgs pair with trace zero and degree
d\in\mathbb{Z}
corresponding to a lifting
(\tilde{P},\tilde{\phi})
of
(P,\phi)
to a
\mathrm{GL}
-Higgs pair. The
degree of

(P,\phi)
is the congruence class
\overline{d}\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}
.

The first homotopy group
\pi_{1}(\mathrm{PGL}(r,\mathbb{C}))
of
\mathrm{PGL}(r,\mathbb{C})
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}
and the degree of
(P,\phi)
characterizes uniquely the topological type of
P
. Moreover, up to the action of multiples of
\mathcal{O}_{C}(1)
on
(E,\Phi)
,the
J(C)
-orbit
[(E,\Phi)]
corresponding to a
\mathrm{PGL}
-Higgs pair with degree
\overline{d}\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}
is the orbit of a Higgs pair of degree
d
with
d\in\{0,\dots,r-1\}
. Then, a
\mathrm{PGL}
-Higgs pair of degree
\overline{d}
is identified uniquely with the orbit in
\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{tr}=0}(r,d)
of a Higgs pair of trace zero
and degree
d
with respect to the action of line bundles
of degree

0
on
C
.

A basis for the invariant polynomials of
\mathfrak{pgl}(r,\mathbb{C})=\mathfrak{sl}(r,\mathbb{C})
is given by
\{p_{2},\dots,p_{r}\}
where
p_{i}(P,\phi):=(-)^{i}\operatorname{tr}(\wedge^{i}\phi)
. Hence, we have the following
\mathrm{PGL}
-Hitchin morphism of rank
r
and degree
\overline{d}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{PGL},r,\overline{d}}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{PGL}}(r,\overline{d})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{PGL}}(r)=\bigoplus_{i=2}^{r}H^{0}(C,L^{i})

\displaystyle(P,\phi)

\displaystyle\longmapsto(p_{2}(P,\phi),\dots,p_{r}(P,\phi)).

Proposition 0.3.4
.

Let
{\underline{a}}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{PGL}}(r)
be any characteristic and let
X=X_{\underline{a}}\xrightarrow{\pi}C
be the associated spectral curve. Let
\overline{d}\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}
with
d\in\{0,\dots,r-1\}
be any degree. The fiber
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{PGL},r,\overline{d}}^{-1}({\underline{a}})
of the
\mathrm{PGL}
-Hitchin morphism is isomorphic, via the spectral correspondence, to the quotient moduli space

\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d^{\prime})/\pi^{*}J^{0}(C)

of torsion-free sheaves of rank 1 and degree
d^{\prime}
up to the action of line bundles of degree
0
on
C
by tensor product, with
d^{\prime}=d+\frac{r(r-1)}{2}\ell
.

The case of
\mathrm{PGL}(r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pairs of degree
0
deserves special attention.

Proposition 0.3.5
.

Let
{\underline{a}}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{PGL}}(r)
be any characteristic, let
X=X_{\underline{a}}\xrightarrow{\pi}C
be the associated spectral curve and denote

B:=\det(\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X})^{-1}
. Let
d^{\prime}=\frac{r(r-1)}{2}\ell
and let
\operatorname{Nm}_{\pi}
be the Norm map induced by
\pi
on
\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d^{\prime})
. Let
J^{0}(C)[r]
be the group stack of line bundles with
r
-th torsion on
C
, acting on
\operatorname{Nm}_{\pi}^{-1}(B)
as follows:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Nm}_{\pi}^{-1}(B)\times\pi^{*}J^{0}(C)[r]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\operatorname{Nm}_{\pi}^{-1}(B)

\displaystyle\big((\mathcal{M},\epsilon),(\pi^{*}N,\pi^{*}(\eta:N^{r}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathcal{O}_{C}))\big)

\displaystyle\longmapsto(\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}N,\epsilon_{N,\eta})

where
\epsilon_{N,\eta}
is equal to the following composition:

\displaystyle\epsilon_{N,\eta}:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Nm}(\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}N)\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\operatorname{Nm}(\mathcal{M})\otimes\operatorname{Nm}(\pi^{*}N)\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\operatorname{Nm}(\mathcal{M})\otimes N^{r}\xrightarrow{\epsilon\otimes\eta}B.

Then, the fiber
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{PGL},r,\overline{0}}^{-1}({\underline{a}})
of the
\mathrm{PGL}
-Hitchin morphism is isomorphic, via the spectral correspondence, to the quotient moduli space

\operatorname{Nm}_{\pi}^{-1}(B)/\pi^{*}(J^{0}C)[r].

0.3.3.
\boldsymbol{G=\mathrm{Sp}(2r,\mathbb{C})}

In this case, the datum of a
\mathrm{Sp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pair
(P,\phi)
on
C
corresponds univocally to the datum of
(E,\Phi,\omega)
where
(E,\Phi)
is a Higgs pair of rank
2r
and degree
0
and
\omega:E\otimes E\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{C}
is a non-degenerate symplectic form on
E
satisfying the condition:

\omega(\Phi v,w)=-\omega(v,\Phi w).

A basis for the invariant polynomials of
\mathfrak{sp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
is given by
\{p_{2i}\}_{i=1,\dots,r}
where
p_{2i}(P,\phi):=\operatorname{tr}(\wedge^{2i}\phi)
. The corresponding
\mathrm{Sp}
-Hitchin morphism takes the form:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{Sp},2r}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(2r):=\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(2r,0)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(2r)=\bigoplus_{l=1}^{r}H^{0}(C,L^{2l})

\displaystyle(P,\phi)

\displaystyle\longmapsto(p_{2}(P,\phi),p_{4}(P,\phi),\dots,p_{2r}(P,\phi)).

For any characteristic
{\underline{a}}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(2r)
, the spectral curve
\pi:X_{\underline{a}}\rightarrow C
is defined in the total space
p:\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{O}_{C}\oplus L^{-1})\rightarrow C
by the equation

x^{2r}y+a_{2}x^{2r-2}y^{2}+\dots+a_{2r-2}x^{2}y^{2r-2}+a_{2r}y^{2r}=0.

Hence, the curve
X_{\underline{a}}
has an involution
\sigma
defined by
\sigma(x)=-x
.

Proposition 0.3.6
.

Let
{\underline{a}}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(r)
be any characteristic and let
X=X_{\underline{a}}\xrightarrow{\pi}C
be the associated spectral curve with involution
\sigma:X\rightarrow X
. The fiber
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{Sp},2r}^{-1}({\underline{a}})
of the
\mathrm{Sp}
-Hitchin morphism is isomorphic, via the spectral correspondence, to the equalizer
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}
of the two maps

\displaystyle\texttt{\char 95\relax}^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}:=\mathcal{H}om(\texttt{\char 95\relax},\mathcal{O}_{X}):\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d^{\prime})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,-d^{\prime})

\displaystyle\sigma^{*}\texttt{\char 95\relax}\otimes\pi^{*}L^{1-2r}:\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d^{\prime})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,-d^{\prime}),

where
d^{\prime}=r(2r-1)\ell
.

0.3.4.
\boldsymbol{G=\mathrm{GSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})}

In this case, the datum of a
\mathrm{GSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pair
(P,\phi)
corresponds uniquely, via the associated bundle construction and the translation of the Higgs field, to the datum
(E,\Phi^{\prime},\omega,M,\mu)
of a Higgs pair
(E,\Phi^{\prime})
of rank
2r
and degree
rd
, a non-degenerate symplectic form
\omega:E\otimes E\rightarrow M
on
E
with values in a line bundle
M
of degree
d
, and a global global section
\mu\in H^{0}(C,L)
, such that
\Phi^{\prime}\in H^{0}(C,\operatorname{End}(E)\otimes L)
satisfies

\omega(\Phi^{\prime}v,w)+\omega(v,\Phi^{\prime}w)=0.

The affine space
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(2r)\oplus H^{0}(C,L)
can be taken as basis of a (translated)
\mathrm{GSp}
-Hitchin morphism
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}
, defined as:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{GSp},2r,rd}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{GSp}}(2r,rd)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(2r)\oplus H^{0}(C,L)

\displaystyle(P,\phi)

\displaystyle\longmapsto({\underline{a}}^{\prime},\mu)=(a_{2}(E,\Phi^{\prime}),a_{4}(E,\Phi^{\prime}),\dots,a_{2r}(E,\Phi^{\prime}),\mu).

where
(E,\Phi)
is the Higgs pair associated to
(P,\phi)
via the associated bundle construction,
\mu
is the global section
\frac{\operatorname{tr}\Phi}{2r}\in H^{0}(C,L)
and
\Phi^{\prime}=\Phi-\mu\id_{E}
is the translated Higgs field.

Proposition 0.3.7
.

Let
{\underline{a}}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(2r)
be any characteristic and let
\mu\in H^{0}(C,L)
be any section. Let
X=X_{{\underline{a}}^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\pi}C

be the spectral curve associated to
{\underline{a}}^{\prime}
, with involution
\sigma:X\rightarrow X
.
Let
d^{\prime}=rd+r(2r-1)\ell
and denote with
\mathcal{P}(d^{\prime},n)=\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d^{\prime})\times J(C,d)
the Cartesian product of the Simpson Jacobian of degree
d^{\prime}
on
X
and the Jacobian of degree
d
on
C
, endowed with the projection maps
p_{X}
and
p_{C}
on
\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d^{\prime})
and
J(C,d)
respectively. Let
\mathcal{E}_{{\underline{a}}^{\prime}}
be the equalizer of the two maps

\displaystyle(\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\texttt{\char 95\relax},\mathcal{O}_{X})\circ p_{X})\otimes(\pi^{*}\circ p_{C}):\mathcal{P}(d^{\prime},d)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,rd-r(2r-1)\ell)

\displaystyle(\sigma^{*}\circ p_{X})\otimes\pi^{*}L^{1-2r}:\mathcal{P}(d^{\prime},d)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,rd-r(2r-1)\ell).

Then, the fiber
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{GSp},2r,rd}^{-1}({\underline{a}}^{\prime},\mu)
is isomorphic, via the spectral correspondence, to
\mathcal{E}_{{\underline{a}}^{\prime}}
.

0.3.5.
\boldsymbol{G=\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})}

In this case, any
\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pair
(P,\phi)
has a lifting
(\tilde{P},\tilde{\phi})
to a
\mathrm{GSp}(r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pair, corresponding via the associated bundle construction to the datum
(E,\Phi,M,\omega)
of a Higgs pair
(E,\Phi)
of rank
2r
and degree
rd
, a non-degenerate symplectic form
\omega:E\otimes E\rightarrow M
with values in a line bundle
M
of degree
d
on
C
, and a Higgs field
\Phi
with trace zero satisfying the condition

\omega(\Phi v,w)+\omega(v,\Phi w)=0.

Then, the datum of
(P,\phi)
corresponds uniquely to the datum of the equivalence class
[(E,\Phi,M,\omega)]
of Higgs pairs of rank
2r
with a non-degenerate symplectic form, under the equivalence relation
\sim_{J(C)}
defined by:

(E,\Phi,M,\omega)\sim_{J(C)}(E\otimes N,\Phi\otimes 1_{N},M\otimes N^{2},\omega_{N})\hskip 20.00003pt\textrm{for any }N\in J(C)

where

\omega_{N}:(E\otimes N)\otimes(E\otimes N)\rightarrow M\otimes N^{2}

is obtained by extension of scalars.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{GSp}}(2r)=\coprod_{d\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{GSp}}(2r,rd)
be the moduli stack of
\mathrm{GSp}
-Higgs pairs of rank
2r
in any degree and denote with
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{GSp}}^{\operatorname{tr}=0}(2r)
the closed substack of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{GSp}}(2r)
given by
\mathrm{GSp}
-Higgs pairs of rank
2r
with trace zero. Then,
[(E,\Phi,M,\omega)]
is the orbit of
(E,\Phi,M,\omega)
under the action of
J(C)
on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{GSp}}^{\operatorname{tr}=0}(2r)
defined by:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{GSp}}^{\operatorname{tr}=0}(2r)\times J(C)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{GSp}}^{\operatorname{tr}=0}(2r)

\displaystyle((E,\Phi,M,\omega),N)

\displaystyle\longmapsto(E\otimes N,\Phi\otimes 1_{N},M\otimes N^{2},\omega_{N}).

Definition 0.3.8
.

Let
(P,\phi)
be a
\mathrm{PSp}
-Higgs pair and let
(E,\Phi,M,\omega)
be the datum of a Higgs pair with trace zero and degree
rd
endowed with a
M
-valued symplectic form
\omega
, corresponding to a lifting
(\tilde{P},\tilde{\phi})
of
(P,\phi)
to a
\mathrm{GSp}
-Higgs pair. The
degree of

(P,\phi)
is the congruence class
\overline{rd}\in\mathbb{Z}/2r\mathbb{Z}
.

Up to the action on
(E,\Phi,M,\omega)
of a line bundle of degree
1
on
C
, it is straightforward to see that the
J(C)
-orbit
[(E,\Phi,M,\omega)]
corresponding to a
\mathrm{PGL}
-Higgs pair with degree
\overline{rd}\in\mathbb{Z}/2r\mathbb{Z}
is always the orbit of a datum whose Higgs pair has degree
0
or
rd
. If we restrict the correspondence to Higgs pairs with fixed degree
0
or
rd
, we see that a
\mathrm{PGL}
-Higgs pair of degree
\overline{rd}
is identified uniquely by the orbit of a Higgs pair with trace zero
and degree
0
or
rd
with respect to the action of line bundles
of degree

0
on
C
.

A basis for the invariant polynomials of
\mathfrak{psp}(2r,\mathbb{C})=\mathfrak{sp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
is given by
\{p_{2i}\}_{i=1,\dots,r}
where
p_{2i}(P,\phi):=\operatorname{tr}(\wedge^{2i}\phi)
. The corresponding
\mathrm{PSp}
-Hitchin morphism takes the form:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{PSp},2r,\overline{rd}}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{PSp}}(2r,\overline{rd})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{PSp}}(2r)=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}H^{0}(C,L^{2l})

\displaystyle(P,\phi)

\displaystyle\longmapsto(p_{2}(P,\phi),p_{4}(P,\phi),\dots,p_{2r}(P,\phi)).

As in the case of
\mathrm{Sp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pairs, the curve
X_{\underline{a}}
has an involution defined by
\sigma(x)=-x
.

Proposition 0.3.9
.

Let
{\underline{a}}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{PSp}}(2r)
be any characteristic, let
X=X_{\underline{a}}\xrightarrow{\pi}C
be the associated spectral curve with involution
\sigma:X\rightarrow X
. Let
d\in\{0,1\}
,
d^{\prime}=rd+r(2r-1)\ell
and denote with
\mathcal{P}(d^{\prime},n)=\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d^{\prime})\times J^{d}(C)
the Cartesian product of the compactified Jacobian of degree
d^{\prime}
on
X
and the Jacobian of degree
d
on
C
, endowed with the projection maps
p_{X}
and
p_{C}
on
\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d^{\prime})
and
J^{d}(C)
respectively. Let
\mathcal{E}_{{\underline{a}}^{\prime}}
be the equalizer of the two maps

\displaystyle(\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\texttt{\char 95\relax},\mathcal{O}_{X})\circ p_{X})\otimes(\pi^{*}\circ p_{C}):\mathcal{P}(d^{\prime},d)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,rd-r(2r-1)\ell)

\displaystyle(\sigma^{*}\circ p_{X})\otimes\pi^{*}L^{1-2r}:\mathcal{P}(d^{\prime},d)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,rd-r(2r-1)\ell).

The group
J^{0}(C)
of line bundles of degree
0
on
C
acts on
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}\times J^{0}(C)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}

\displaystyle((\mathcal{M},M,\lambda),N)

\displaystyle\longmapsto(\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}N,M\otimes N^{2},\lambda_{N})

where
\lambda_{N}
is given by the composition of
\lambda\otimes\id_{\pi^{*}N}
with the canonical isomorphisms:

\displaystyle(\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}N)^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}\otimes\pi^{*}(M\otimes N^{2})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathcal{M}^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}\otimes(\pi^{*}N)^{-1}\otimes\pi^{*}M\otimes(\pi^{*}N)^{2}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathcal{M}^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}\otimes\pi^{*}M\otimes\pi^{*}N\xrightarrow{\lambda\otimes\id_{\pi^{*}N}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\sigma^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}L^{1-2r}\otimes\pi^{*}N\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\sigma^{*}(\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}N)\otimes\pi^{*}L^{1-2r}.

Then, the fiber
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{PSp},2r,\overline{rd}}^{-1}({\underline{a}})
of the
\mathrm{PSp}
-Hitchin morphism is isomorphic, via the spectral correspondence, to the quotient
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}/J^{0}(C)
.

The case of
\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pairs of degree
0
deserves special attention.

Proposition 0.3.10
.

Let
{\underline{a}}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{PSp}}(2r)
be any characteristic, let
X=X_{\underline{a}}\xrightarrow{\pi}C
be the associated spectral curve with involution
\sigma:X\rightarrow X
. Let
d^{\prime}=r(2r-1)\ell
and let
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}
be the equalizer stack of the two maps:

\displaystyle\texttt{\char 95\relax}^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}:=\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\texttt{\char 95\relax},\mathcal{O}_{X}):\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d^{\prime})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,-d^{\prime})

\displaystyle\sigma^{*}\texttt{\char 95\relax}\otimes\pi^{*}L^{1-2r}:\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d^{\prime})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,-d^{\prime})

The group stack
J^{0}(C)[2]
of 2-torsion line bundles on
C
acts on
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}\times\pi^{*}J^{0}(C)[2]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}

\displaystyle((\mathcal{M},\lambda),(\pi^{*}N,\pi^{*}\epsilon))

\displaystyle\longmapsto(\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}N,\lambda\otimes\pi^{*}\epsilon).

Then, the fiber
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{PSp},2r,\overline{0}}^{-1}({\underline{a}})
of the
\mathrm{PSp}
-Hitchin morphism is isomorphic, via the spectral correspondence, to the quotient
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}/\pi^{*}J^{0}(C)[2]
.

Chapter 1
Preliminaries

1.1.
Rank and degree of coherent sheaves

Let
X
be any Noetherian scheme of pure dimension
1
. In this section, we study classes of coherent sheaves on
X
for which the notions of rank on
X
are well defined. Moreover, we discuss the notions of degree for sheaves with well-defined rank.

The simplest class is clearly given by vector bundles or, equivalently, locally free sheaves of constant rank. If
\mathcal{F}
is a locally free sheaf on
X
, we say that
\mathcal{F}
has
(free) rank

r
if
\mathcal{F}_{x}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{X,x}^{\oplus r}
for any point
x
of
X
.

For any coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
X
, we give now the following definitions.

Definition 1.1.1
.

The
support
of
\mathcal{F}
is the closed set

\operatorname{Supp}(\mathcal{F})=\{x\in X|\mathcal{F}_{x}\neq 0\}\subseteq X.

The
dimension
of
\mathcal{F}
is the dimension of its support and is denoted
\dim(\mathcal{F})
.

Definition 1.1.2
.

The
torsion subsheaf
of
\mathcal{F}
is the maximal subsheaf
T(\mathcal{F})\subseteq\mathcal{F}
of dimension 0. If
\mathcal{F}=T(\mathcal{F})
, we say that
\mathcal{F}
is a
torsion sheaf
. If
T(\mathcal{F})=0
we say that
\mathcal{F}
is
torsion-free
.

Equivalently,
\mathcal{F}
is torsion-free if
\dim(\mathcal{E})=1
for any non-trivial coherent subsheaf
\mathcal{E}\subseteq\mathcal{F}
, i.e.
\mathcal{F}
is pure of dimension 1. For any sheaf
\mathcal{F}
, the quotient sheaf
\mathcal{F}/T(\mathcal{F})
is either zero or torsion-free.

The first class of torsion-free sheaves with well-defined rank is given by generalized vector bundles.

Definition 1.1.3
.

\mathcal{F}
is a
generalized vector bundle
if it is torsion-free and there exists a positive integer
r
such that
\mathcal{F}_{\xi}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\xi}^{\oplus r}
for any generic point
\xi
of
X
, where
\mathcal{F}_{\xi}
denotes the stalk of
\mathcal{F}
at
\xi
. The integer
r
is called the
rank
of the generalized vector bundle
\mathcal{F}
. A generalized vector bundle of rank
1
is also called a
generalized line bundle
.

Clearly, a vector bundle of rank
r
is also a generalized vector bundle of the same rank.

Remark 1.1.4
.

Generalized line bundles were introduced in
[
BE00
]
in order to study limit linear series on a ribbon. As pointed out in
[
EG95
]
, generalized line bundles are strictly related to generalized divisors introduced indipendently by Hartshone in
[
Har86
]
(followed by
[
Har94
]
and
[
Har07
]
). See also Chapter
2
,
2.2
for details. The generalization to any rank
r
is due to
[
Sav19
]
.

We introduce now the notion of rank for any coherent sheaf. Denote with
X_{1},\dots,X_{s}
the irreducible components of
X
and with
\xi_{i}
the generic point of each irreducible component
X_{i}
. Denote with
C_{i}=X_{i,red}
the reduced subscheme underlying
X_{i}
for each
i
.

Definition 1.1.5
.

The
multiplicity
of
C_{i}
in
X
is defined as the positive integer:

\operatorname{mult}_{X}(C_{i}):=\ell_{\mathcal{O}_{X,\xi_{i}}}(\mathcal{O}_{X,\xi_{i}}).

where
\ell_{\mathcal{O}_{X,\xi_{i}}}
denotes the length as
\mathcal{O}_{X,\xi_{i}}
-module.

Definition 1.1.6
.

(Rank and multirank of a coherent sheaf)
The
rank
of
\mathcal{F}
on
X_{i}
is defined as the positive rational number

\operatorname{rk}_{X_{i}}(\mathcal{F})=\frac{\ell_{\mathcal{O}_{X,\xi_{i}}}(\mathcal{F}_{\xi_{i}})}{\operatorname{mult}_{X}(C_{i})}.

The
multirank
of
\mathcal{F}
on
X
is the
n
-uple
{\underline{r}}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{n})
where
r_{i}
is the rank of
\mathcal{F}
at
X_{i}
. The sheaf
\mathcal{F}
has
rank

r
on
X
if it has rank
r
at each
X_{i}
.

Note that, since length of modules is additive in short exact sequences, the rank and multirank of coherent sheaves are additive in short exact sequences too.

Proposition 1.1.7
.

A generalized vector bundle of rank
r
on
X
has rank
r
as a coherent sheaf. If
X
is reduced, any torsion-free sheaf of rank
r
is a generalized vector bundle of the same rank.

Proof.

The first statement follows from
1.1.6
and the fact that isomorphic modules have the same length. For the second assertion, note that on an integral curve
X
there exists for any torsion-free sheaf
\mathcal{F}
an open dense subset
U\subset X
such that
\mathcal{F}_{|U}
is locally free, hence the stalk of
\mathcal{F}
at the generic point of
X
is a free
\mathcal{O}_{X,\xi}
-module of rank
\operatorname{rk}_{X}(\mathcal{F})
. If
X
is reducible, the assertion follows by considering any irreducible component.
∎

Remark 1.1.8
.

There are in literature other notions for the rank of a coherent sheaf on a irreducible component. A classical notion is the
reduced rank
of
\mathcal{F}
on
X_{i}
:

\operatorname{\textbf{rank}}_{X_{i}}(\mathcal{F})=\dim_{\kappa(\xi_{i})}(\mathcal{F}_{\xi_{i}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X,\xi}}\kappa(\xi_{i})).

This definition computes the rank of the sheaf restricted to its reduced support; it agrees with the other definitions for generalized vector bundles. However, there are cases when the reduced rank of a torsion-free sheaf differs from its rank, as it happens for quasi-locally free sheaves on a ribbon (see
[
Sav19
, §1.4]
). They also provide examples of torsion-free sheaves which are not generalized vector bundles, even if their rank is well defined.

The definition of
\operatorname{rk}_{H}(\mathcal{F})
comes at least from
[
Sch98
, Définition 1.2]
, where it is given in the context of projective
k
-schemes without embedded points. The notion of
generalized rank
introduced in
[
Dre04
]
and
[
Sav19
]
is essentially equivalent. Finally, a common definition in projective algebraic geometry is the polarized rank, that we will discuss later on in the present section.

In order to introduce the degree of coherent sheaves, from now on we
assume that
X
is projective curve over a base field
k
. Recall that the Euler characteristic of a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
is

\chi(\mathcal{F}):=\sum(-)^{i}\dim_{k}H^{i}(X,\mathcal{F}).

Definition 1.1.9
.

(Degree of a coherent sheaf)
Suppose that
\mathcal{F}
has rank
r
on
X
. Then, the
degree
of
\mathcal{F}
on
X
is defined as the fractional number:

\deg\mathcal{F}=\chi(X,\mathcal{F})-r\chi(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}).

Remark 1.1.10
.

If the rank of
\mathcal{F}
is integer, the degree is integer as well. If
X
is integral, the degree of a sheaf is an integer and coincides with the classical notion of degree for sheaves on integral curves.

The following technical lemma is very useful.

Lemma 1.1.11
.

Assume that
X
is irreducible. Let
\mathcal{F}
be a coherent sheaf of rank
r
on
X
and let
\mathcal{E}
be a locally free sheaf of rank
n
on
X
. Then

\chi(X,\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{E})=r\deg(\mathcal{E})+n\chi(X,\mathcal{F}).

Proof.

The proof is inspired by
[
Sta19
,
\href
https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/0AYVTag 0AYV]
and uses devissage for coherent sheaves as stated in
[
Sta19
,
\href
https://stacks.math.columbia.edu/tag/01YITag 01YI]
. Let
\mathcal{P}
be the property of coherent sheaves
\mathcal{F}
on
X
expressing that the formula of the Lemma holds. By additivity of rank and Euler characteristic in short exact sequences,
\mathcal{P}
satisfy the two-out-of-three property. The integral subschemes
Z
of
X
are the reduced subscheme
C
with support equal to
X
, and the closed points of
X
. For
Z=C
, the formula of the Lemma for
\mathcal{O}_{C}
is:

\chi(X,\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C})=\frac{\deg(\mathcal{E})}{\operatorname{mult}_{X}C}+n\chi(X,\mathcal{O}_{C}).

This is true by definition of degree of
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}=\mathcal{E}_{|C}
on
C
and the fact that
\deg(\mathcal{E})=\operatorname{mult}_{X}(C)\deg(\mathcal{E}_{|C})
. Then
\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{O}_{C})
holds. If
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
is a closed point, the formula of the Lemma is true for
i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z}
since it is a torsion sheaf of rank 0 and
\chi(X,\mathcal{E}\otimes i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z})=n\chi(X,i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z})
. Then
\mathcal{P}(i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Z})
holds.
∎

Let
H
be any polarization of
X
; then, the notion of polarized rank and degree of sheaves can be defined. Denote with
H_{|C_{i}}
the restriction of
H
to the closed subscheme
C_{i}\subseteq X
. Since
H
is locally free of rank
1
, the degree of
H
on
X
is related to the degrees of each
H_{|C_{i}}
on
C_{i}
by the formula:

\deg H=\sum_{i=1}^{s}\operatorname{mult}_{X}(C_{i})\deg H_{|C_{i}}.

Definition 1.1.12
.

(Polarized rank and degree of a torsion-free sheaf)
Let
H
be any polarization of
X
with degree
\deg H=\delta
and let
\mathcal{F}
be a torsion-free sheaf on
X
. The
polarized rank and degree
of
\mathcal{F}
are the rational numbers
r_{H}(\mathcal{F})
and
d_{H}(\mathcal{F})
determined by the Hilbert polinomial of
\mathcal{F}
with respect to
H
:

P(\mathcal{F},n,H):=\chi(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(nH))=\delta r_{H}(\mathcal{F})n+d_{H}(\mathcal{F})+r_{H}(\mathcal{F})\chi(\mathcal{O}_{X}).

The polarized rank and degree of a sheaf depend strictly on the degrees of the restrictions
H_{|C_{i}}
, as the following theorem shows.

Theorem 1.1.13
.

Let
H
be a polarization of
X
and let
\mathcal{F}
be a torsion-free sheaf on
X
. The polarized rank of
\mathcal{F}
is related to the multirank of
\mathcal{F}
by the formula

r_{H}(\mathcal{F})=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{s}\operatorname{rk}_{X_{i}}(\mathcal{F})\operatorname{mult}_{X}(C_{i})\deg H_{|C_{i}}}{\sum_{i=1}^{s}\operatorname{mult}_{X}(C_{i})\deg H_{|C_{i}}}.

Proof.

Since the Hilbert polynomial of
\mathcal{F}
with respect to
H
has degree
1
, its leading term can be computed in terms of Euler characteristic as

\chi(X,\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(nH))-\chi(X,\mathcal{F}).

Since
\delta=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\operatorname{mult}_{X}(C_{i})\deg_{C_{i}}H
is the degree of
H
on
X
, by Definition
1.1.12
we have to prove:

\chi(X,\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(nH))-\chi(X,\mathcal{F})=n\sum_{i=1}^{s}\operatorname{rk}_{X_{i}}(\mathcal{F})\operatorname{mult}_{X}(C_{i})\deg_{C_{i}}H.

First, we reduce to the case of
X
irreducible. Consider the exact sequence:

0\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i}\mathcal{F}_{|X_{i}}\rightarrow T\rightarrow 0

where
T
is a torsion sheaf supported only at the intersections of the irreducible components. Tensoring by
nH
, we obtain another exact sequence:

0\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(nH)\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(nH))_{|X_{i}}\rightarrow T\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(nH)\rightarrow 0.

By additivity of the Euler characteristic with respect to exact sequences we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{i}

\displaystyle\left(\chi(X_{i},(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(nH))_{|X_{i}})-\chi(X_{i},\mathcal{F}_{|X_{i}})\right)=

\displaystyle=\chi(X,\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(nH))-\chi(X,\mathcal{F})+\chi(X,T\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(nH))-\chi(X,T).

Since
T
has dimension 0, the characteristics
\chi(X,T\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(nH))
and
\chi(X,T)
are the same, hence:

\chi(X,\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(nH))-\chi(X,\mathcal{F})=\sum_{i}\left(\chi(X_{i},(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(nH))_{|X_{i}})-\chi(X_{i},\mathcal{F}_{|X_{i}})\right).

We are then reduced to prove the statement on each irreducible component
X_{i}
. In other words we have to prove that, if
X
is a irreducible (possibly non-reduced) curve with reduced structure
C
, then:

\chi(X,\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(nH))-\chi(X,\mathcal{F})=n\deg_{X}(H)\operatorname{rk}_{X}(\mathcal{F}).

This is exactly the content of Lemma
1.1.11
, with
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{O}_{X}(nH)
.
∎

Corollary 1.1.14
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be any torsion-free sheaf with well-defined rank. Then, its polarized rank
r_{H}(\mathcal{F})
and degree
d_{H}(\mathcal{F})
are equal respectively to
\operatorname{rk}_{X}(\mathcal{F})
and
\deg_{X}(\mathcal{F})
for any polarization
H
on
X
. f
X
is irreducible, the rank and degree of a sheaf coincide with the polarized counterparts, for any polarization.

Proof.

The statement about the rank follows from Theorem
1.1.13
, where
r_{i}=r
for each irreducible component
X_{i}
. To prove the statement about the degree, note that for any polarization
H
:

d_{H}(\mathcal{F})=\chi(X,\mathcal{F})-r_{H}(\mathcal{F})\chi(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}).

If
r_{H}(\mathcal{F})=\operatorname{rk}(\mathcal{F})
, then
d_{H}(\mathcal{F})=\deg(\mathcal{F})
by Definition
1.1.9
. The last statement follows from the fact that the
\operatorname{rk}_{X}(\mathcal{F})
is well-defined for any torsion-free sheaf when
X
has only one irreducible component.
∎

Remark 1.1.15
.

For reducible curves, the definitions of polarized rank and degree are the most general. As pointed out in
[
Lop04
, §2]
, being of polarized rank
1
for a torsion-free sheaf does not ensure that the sheaf is supported on the whole curve. Moreover, there are cases of torsion-free sheaves of polarized rank
1
whose polarized rank on the restrictions to the irreducible components is different from
1
and not an integer, even for reduced curves.

1.2.
Moduli spaces of torsion-free sheaves “of rank 1”

In this section,
X
is a (possibly reducible, non-reduced) projective curve over a base field
k
, with irreducible components
X_{1},\dots,X_{s}
. We introduce the definition of several moduli spaces parametrizing classes of torsion-free sheaves “of rank 1” (with different meanings) that will play an important role in the present work. We start by recalling the definition of the moduli space parametrizing line bundles on
X
.

Definition 1.2.1
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{Z}
be an integer number. The
Jacobian scheme
of degree
d
on
X
is the algebraic scheme
J^{d}(X)
representing the sheafification of the functor that associates to any
k
-scheme
T
the set of isomorphism classes of line bundles of degree
d
on
X\times_{k}T
. The union of the Jacobians of all degrees is denoted as
J(X)
.

Remark 1.2.2
.

The existence of the representing scheme
J^{d}(X)
is a classical result in algebraic geometry, see for example
[
Kle05
, Corollary 4.18.3]
. Following a number of authors (see for example
[
MRV17a
]
or
[
Kas15
]
) we prefer to use the term “Jacobian” instead of “Picard” even if we consider line bundles in any degree; this is motivated only by the simpler notation.

Since we are dealing with moduli spaces, we recall also the definition of slope and stability for torsion-free sheaves.

Definition 1.2.3
.

Let
H
be a polarization on
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{F}
be a torsion-free sheaf on
X
with polarized rank
r_{H}(\mathcal{F})
and polarized degree
d_{H}(\mathcal{F})
.

The
slope
of
\mathcal{F}
with respect to
H
is defined as the rational number
\mu_{H}(\mathcal{F})=d_{H}(\mathcal{F})/r_{H}(\mathcal{F})
.

A coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
X
is
H
-
stable
(respectively
H
-
semi-stable
) if it is torsion-free and for any proper subsheaf
\mathcal{E}\subset\mathcal{F}
the equality
\mu_{H}(\mathcal{E})<\mu_{H}(\mathcal{F})
holds (respectively
\leq
).

We start from the more general moduli space, parametrizing for torsion-free sheaves of polarized rank 1.

Definition/Lemma 1.2.4
.

Let
H
be a polarization on
X
and let
d\in\mathbb{Z}
be an integer number. The
Simpson Jacobian stack
of degree
d
on
X
is the algebraic stack
\overline{\mathbb{J}}_{tf}(X,H,d)
such that for any
k
-scheme
T
,
\overline{\mathbb{J}}_{tf}(X,d)(T)
is the groupoid of
T
-flat coherent sheaves on
X\times_{k}T
whose fibers over
T
are torsion-free sheaves of polarized rank 1 and polarized degree
d
on
X\simeq X\times_{k}\{t\}
with respect to
H
.

The
Simpson Jacobian
of degree
d
is the projective scheme
\overline{J}_{tf}(X,H,d)
which is the good moduli space representing S-equivalence classes of
H
-semistable torsion-free sheaves of polarized rank 1 and polarized degree
d
on
X
.

The union of the Simpson Jacobians of all degrees is denoted
\overline{\mathbb{J}}_{tf}(X,H)
(resp.
\overline{J}_{tf}(X,H)
).

Proof.

The algebraicity of
\overline{\mathbb{J}}_{tf}(X,H,d)
follows from the algebraicity of the stack of coherent sheaves on
X
(see
[
Lie06
, 2.1]
and
[
CW17
, Theorem 7.20]
). The moduli space of semistable sheaves with fixed polarized rank and degree was constructed by Simpson in
[
Sim94a
]
.
∎

In the following proposition, we show that the multirank of sheaves splits
\overline{\mathbb{J}}_{tf}(X,H,d)
in unions of connected components.

Lemma 1.2.5
.

Let
H
be a fixed polarization on
X
. For any
{\underline{r}}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{s})\in\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}^{s}
, let
W_{\underline{r}}\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{J}}_{tf}(X,H,d)
be the substack parametrizing torsion-free sheaves with multirank equal to
{\underline{r}}
. Then
\{W_{\underline{r}}|W_{\underline{r}}\neq\emptyset\}_{{\underline{r}}\in\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}^{s}}
is a finite collection of pairwise disjoint substacks of
\overline{\mathbb{J}}_{tf}(X,H,d)
that covers the whole space. Moreover, each
W_{\underline{r}}
is open and closed, hence union of connected components of
\overline{\mathbb{J}}_{tf}(X,H,d)
.

Proof.

The multi-rank of a sheaf is well-defined, hence the substacks
W_{\underline{r}}
are pairwise disjoint.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a sheaf with polarized rank
r_{H}
equal to
1
. By Theorem
1.1.13
its multirank
{\underline{r}}
must satisfy:

1=r_{H}(\mathcal{F})=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{s}r_{i}(t)\operatorname{mult}_{X}(C_{i})\deg_{C_{i}}H}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\operatorname{mult}_{X}(C_{i})\deg_{C_{i}}H}.

Since each
r_{i}
is a non-negative fraction with integer numerator and denominator equal to
\operatorname{mult}_{X}(C_{i})
, the possible values for
{\underline{r}}
form a finite subset of
\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}^{s}
, once that
H
and
r_{H}
are fixed. Then, only a finite number of
W_{\underline{r}}
is non-empty, hence
\{W_{\underline{r}}|W_{\underline{r}}\neq\emptyset\}
is a finite collection.

We claim that each
W_{\underline{r}}
is closed; since the collection is finite and the
W_{\underline{r}}
’s are pairwise disjoint, this implies that each
W_{\underline{r}}
is open and closed, and hence union of connected components.

To prove that
W_{\underline{r}}
is closed, let
T
be any
k
-scheme and let
\mathcal{F}
be any family of torsion-free sheaves of polarized rank
1
on
X\times_{k}T\xrightarrow{\pi}T
. Suppose that
\mathcal{F}
has generically multirank
{\underline{r}}
on
X
, meaning that
\operatorname{rk}_{X_{i}}(\mathcal{F}_{|\pi^{-1}(\eta)})=r_{i}
for each irreducible component
X_{i}
of
X
and any generic point
\eta
of
T
.
Let
t
be any point in the closure of
\{\eta\}
. Since the length is upper-semicontinuous, the rank of
\mathcal{F}_{|\pi^{-1}(t)}
at each
X_{i}
is a rational number
r_{i}(t)
greater or equal than
r_{i}
. On the other hand,
\mathcal{F}_{|\pi^{-1}(t)}
have polarized rank
1
; hence, thanks to Theorem
1.1.13
, we can write:

\displaystyle 1=r_{H}(\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{s}r_{i}(t)\operatorname{mult}_{X}(C_{i})\deg_{C_{i}}H}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\operatorname{mult}_{X}(C_{i})\deg_{C_{i}}H}\geq

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{s}r_{i}\operatorname{mult}_{X}(C_{i})\deg_{C_{i}}H}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\operatorname{mult}_{X}(C_{i})\deg_{C_{i}}H}=1.

By difference we obtain:

\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{s}(r_{i}(t)-r_{i})\operatorname{mult}_{X}(C_{i})\deg_{C_{i}}H}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\operatorname{mult}_{X}(C_{i})\deg_{C_{i}}H}=0.

Since all the terms of the expression are non-negative, the only possibility is that
r_{i}(t)=r_{i}
for each
i
. We conclude that the whole family
\mathcal{F}
belongs to
W_{\underline{r}}
.
∎

We come now to torsion-free sheaves with well-defined rank 1 on
X
.

Definition/Lemma 1.2.6
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{Z}
be an integer number. The
compactified Jacobian stack
of degree
d
on
X
is the algebraic stack
\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d)
such that for any
k
-scheme
T
,
\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d)(T)
is the groupoid of
T
-flat coherent sheaves on
X\times_{k}T
whose fibers over
T
are torsion-free sheaves of rank 1 and degree
d
on
X\simeq X\times_{k}\{t\}
.

Let
H
be a polarization on
X
. The
compactified Jacobian scheme
of degree
d
is the good moduli space
\overline{J}(X,H,d)
representing S-equivalence classes of
H
-semistable torsion-free sheaves of rank 1 and degree
d
on
X
.

The union of the compactified Jacobians of all degrees is denoted
\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X)
(resp.
\overline{J}(X,H)
).

Proof.

The property of having rank
1
on
X
implies that the polarized rank is equal to
1
for any polarization; moreover, the degree is well defined and equal to the polarized degree for any polarization. Fix any polarization
H
on
X
. Then,
\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d)
can be seen as the open and closed substack
W_{(1,\dots,1)}
of
\overline{\mathbb{J}}_{tf}(X,H,d)
(Lemma
1.2.5
), and hence is algebraic. The same argument holds for
\overline{J}(X,H,d)
, with the difference that the notion of semi-stability strictly depends on the choice of
H
.
∎

The following proposition shows that
\overline{J}(X,H,d)
is actually a compactification of
J^{d}(X)
.

Proposition 1.2.7
.

For any polarization
H
on
X
,
\overline{J}(X,H,d)
is a projective scheme, union of connected components of
\overline{J}_{tf}(X,H,d)
, and contains
J^{d}(X)
as an open subscheme.

Proof.

Since
\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d)
is an open and closed substack of
\overline{\mathbb{J}}_{tf}(X,H,d)
, then
\overline{J}(X,H,d)
is an open and closed subscheme of
\overline{J}_{tf}(X,H,d)
and union of connected components. It is non-empty, as it contains the structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{X}
. Moreover
\overline{J}_{tf}(X,H,d)
is projective, hence
\overline{J}(X,H,d)
is projective too. Finally, the condition of being locally free is open and implies both stability and rank 1; hence,
J^{d}(X)
is an open subscheme of
\overline{J}(X,H,d)
.
∎

We come now to the last moduli space, which parametrizes generalized line bundles.

Definition/Lemma 1.2.8
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{Z}
be an integer number. The
generalized Jacobian stack
of degree
d
on
X
is the algebraic stack
\operatorname{\textit{G}\mathbb{J}}(X,d)
such that, for any
k
-scheme
T
,
\operatorname{\textit{G}\mathbb{J}}(X,d)(T)
is the groupoid of
T
-flat coherent sheaves on
X\times_{k}T
whose fibers over
T
are generalized line bundles of degree
d
on
X\simeq X\times_{k}\{t\}
.

Let
H
be a polarization on
X
. The
generalized Jacobian scheme
of degree
d
is the good moduli space
\operatorname{\textit{GJ}}(X,H,d)
representing S-equivalence classes of
H
-semistable generalized line bundles of degree
d
on
X
.

The union of the generalized Jacobians of all degrees is denoted
\operatorname{\textit{G}\mathbb{J}}(X)
(resp.
\operatorname{\textit{GJ}}(X,H)
).

Proof.

Generalized line bundles are torsion-free sheaves of rank 1; moreover, the condition of being generically locally free is open, hence
\operatorname{\textit{G}\mathbb{J}}(X,d)
can be seen as an open substack of
\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d)
. In particular,
\operatorname{\textit{G}\mathbb{J}}(X,d)
is an algebraic stack. Similarly,
\operatorname{\textit{GJ}}(X,H,d)
is an open subscheme of
\overline{J}(X,H,d)
.
∎

To sum up, we defined a number of moduli spaces satisfying the following chain of inclusions:

J^{d}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1)}}{{\subseteq}}\operatorname{\textit{G}\mathbb{J}}(X,d)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2)}}{{\subseteq}}\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(3)}}{{\subseteq}}\overline{\mathbb{J}}_{tf}(X,H,d),

and:

J^{d}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1^{\prime})}}{{\subseteq}}\operatorname{\textit{GJ}}(X,H,d)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2^{\prime})}}{{\subseteq}}\overline{J}(X,H,d)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(3^{\prime})}}{{\subseteq}}\overline{J}_{tf}(X,H,d).

Remark 1.2.9
.

Inclusions
(1)
and
(2)
(resp.
(1^{\prime})
and
(2^{\prime})
) above are open embeddings; inclusion
(3)
(resp.
(3^{\prime})
) is an open and closed embedding. In general they are strict and not dense, in the sense that for any of them there exists a (possibly non-reduced or reducible) curve
X
such that the closure of the smaller space in the bigger one is contained strictly. Anyway, when
X
satisfies additional conditions, some of the inclusions above are actually equalities.

•

If
X
is irreducible, inclusion
(3)
(resp.
(3)^{\prime}
) is an equality.

•

If
X
is reduced, inclusion
(2)
(resp.
(2^{\prime})
) is an equality.

•

If
X
is integral and smooth, inclusions
(1)
,
(2)
and
(3)
(resp.
(1^{\prime})
,
(2^{\prime})
and
(3^{\prime})
) are equalities.

1.3.
Moduli spaces of Higgs pairs

In this section, following
[
Sch14
, Chapter 2]
and
[
MRV17b
, §10]
, we review the definitions of Higgs pairs and
G
-Higgs pairs and of the fibrations involving their moduli spaces. Throughout this section,
C
denotes a fixed smooth curve over the field of complex numbers and
L
a fixed line bundle on
C
with degree
\ell=\deg L
.

1.3.1.
Classical Higgs pairs

We begin with the case of classical Higgs pairs.

Definition 1.3.1
.

A
(
L
-twisted) Higgs pair
(or simply a
Higgs pair
) is a pair
(E,\Phi)
consisting of a vector bundle
E
on
C
and a section
\Phi\in H^{0}(\Sigma,\operatorname{End}(E)\otimes L)
. The map
\Phi
is called the
Higgs field
. The degree and the rank of the Higgs pair are the degree and the rank of the underlying bundle
E
.

Definition 1.3.2
.

The algebraic stack
\mathcal{M}(r,d)
of all
L
-twisted Higgs pairs
(E,\Phi)
on
C
of rank
r
and degree
d
is endowed with a morphism, called the
Hitchin morphism
, defined as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{r,d}=\mathcal{H}:\mathcal{M}(r,d)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathcal{A}(r)=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}H^{0}(C,L^{i})

\displaystyle(E,\Phi)

\displaystyle\mapsto(a_{1}(E,\Phi),\dots,a_{r}(E,\Phi))

where
L^{i}=L^{\otimes i}
,
a_{i}(E,\Phi):=(-1)^{i}\operatorname{tr}(\wedge^{i}\Phi)
and
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}(r)
is called the
Hitchin base
.

Definition 1.3.3
.

A vector subbundle
F\subseteq E
such that
\Phi(F)\subseteq F\otimes L
is said to be a
\Phi
-
invariant subbundle
of
E
.

A Higgs pair
(E,\Phi)
is said
stable
(respectively
semi-stable
) if for each proper
\Phi
-invariant subbundle
F\subset E
one has
\mu(F)<\mu(E)
(respectively
\mu(F)\leq\mu(E)
).

Definition 1.3.4
.

The good moduli space
M(r,d)
of S-equivalence classes of semistable
L
-twisted Higgs pairs of rank
r
and degree
d
is endowed with a flat projective morphism, called the
Hitchin fibration
:

\displaystyle H_{r,d}=H:M(r,d)=M

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}(r)=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}H^{0}(C,L^{i})

\displaystyle(E,\Phi)

\displaystyle\mapsto(a_{1}(E,\Phi),\dots,a_{r}(E,\Phi)).

1.3.2.
G-Higgs pairs

Let
G
be a complex reductive Lie group.

Definition 1.3.5
.

A
(
L
-twisted)
G
-Higgs pair
is a pair
(P,\phi)
where
P
is a principal
G
-bundle over
C
and the
G
-Higgs field
\phi
is a section of
H^{0}(\ad(P)\otimes L)
.

Recall 1.3.6
.

When
G\xhookrightarrow{\rho}\mathrm{GL}(r,\mathbb{C})
, to any
G
-principal bundle
P
is associated a vector bundle of rank
r
, defined as:

E:=P\times_{\rho}\mathbb{C}^{r}=(P\times\mathbb{C}^{r})/\sim

where
(s\cdot g,v)\sim(s,\rho(g)\cdot v)
for any section
s
of
P
and any vector
v\in\mathbb{C}^{r}
. In terms of cocycles, if
\{U_{\alpha}\}_{A}
is a trivializing cover for
P
on
C
and
\{g_{\alpha\beta}\}
is a collection of transition elements, then
E
is the vector bundle defined by the
\rho(g_{\alpha\beta})
as cocycles.

When,
G=\mathrm{GL}(r,\mathbb{C})
the construction can be reversed; indeed, given any vector bundle
E
of rank
r
on
C
, the frame bundle of all ordered basis
P=\operatorname{Fr}(E)
is a
\mathrm{GL}(r,\mathbb{C})
-principal bundle such that
E\simeq P\times_{\rho}\mathbb{C}^{r}
.

Recall 1.3.7
.

Let
P
be any
G
-principal bundle and
E=P\times_{\rho}\mathbb{C}^{r}
as above. The adjoint bundle
\ad(P)
is defined as
P\times_{\ad_{G}}\mathfrak{g}
where
\mathfrak{g}=\operatorname{Lie}(G)
is the Lie algebra associated to the Lie group
G
and
\ad_{G}:G\rightarrow\operatorname{Aut}(\mathfrak{g})
is the adjoint representation. If
G\xhookrightarrow{\rho}\mathrm{GL}(r,\mathbb{C})
, then the embedding
\rho
induces at the level of Lie algebras a
G
-equivariant map
\psi:=\ad\rho:\mathfrak{g}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{gl}(r,\mathbb{C})=\operatorname{End}(\mathbb{C}^{r})
; hence, we obtain a morphism

\ad P=P\times_{\ad_{G}}\mathfrak{g}\xrightarrow{1\times\psi}P\times_{\psi(\ad_{G})}\operatorname{End}(\mathbb{C}^{r})=\operatorname{End}(E).

When
G=\mathrm{GL}(r,\mathbb{C})
, the adjoint bundle
\ad P
is canonically isomorphic to
\operatorname{End}(E)
.

The two remarks above allows us to formulate the following:

Proposition 1.3.8
.

Let
G\xhookrightarrow{\rho}\mathrm{GL}(r,\mathbb{C})
be a complex reductive Lie group emebedding in
\mathrm{GL}(r,\mathbb{C})
. Then, any
G
-Higgs pair
(P,\phi)
gives rise via the associated bundle construction to a classical Higgs pair
(E,\Phi)
of rank
r
, where
E=P\times_{\rho}\mathbb{C}^{r}
and
\Phi
is the image of
\phi
with respect to the morphism
\ad(P)\rightarrow\operatorname{End}(E)
induced by
\rho
.

When
G=\mathrm{GL}(r,\mathbb{C})
,
G
-Higgs pairs are in one-to-one correspondence, via the associated bundle construction, with classical Higgs pairs of rank
r
.

Similarly to Higgs pairs, the moduli space of
G
-Higgs pairs admits a morphism to an affine space, which plays an important role in its study.

Definition 1.3.9
.

Let
G
be a complex reductive Lie group, let
\delta\in\pi_{1}(G)
and let
p_{1},\dots,p_{k}
be a homogeneous basis for the algebra of invariant polynomials of
the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
of
G
, such that
p_{i}
has degree
d_{i}
. Then, the algebraic moduli stack
\mathcal{M}_{G}(\delta)
of all
L
-twisted
G
-Higgs pairs
(P,\phi)
such that
P
has topological type
\delta
is endowed with a morphism, called the
G
-
Hitchin morphism
, defined as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{G,\delta}^{p_{1},\dots,p_{k}}=\mathcal{H}_{G,\delta}:\mathcal{M}_{G}(\delta)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{G}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}H^{0}(C,L^{d_{i}})

\displaystyle(P,\phi)

\displaystyle\mapsto(p_{1}(\phi),\dots,p_{k}(\phi)).

When
G\xhookrightarrow{\rho}\mathrm{GL}(r,\mathbb{C})
, the associated bundle construction induces a morphism of algebraic stack:

\mathcal{M}_{G}(\delta)\xrightarrow{\alpha}\mathcal{M}(r,d).

where
d
corresponds to
\delta
by the canonical map
\pi_{1}(G)\xrightarrow{\pi_{1}(\rho)}\pi_{1}(\mathrm{GL}(r,\mathbb{C}))
.

Definition 1.3.10
.

Let
G\xhookrightarrow{\rho}\mathrm{GL}(r,\mathbb{C})
. We denote as

\mathcal{M}_{G}(r,d):=\coprod_{\pi_{1}(\rho)(\delta)=d}\mathcal{M}_{G}(\delta)

the moduli space of
G
-Higgs pairs whose associated vector bundle is of rank
r
and degree
d
. The
G
-Hitchin morphism on
\mathcal{M}_{G}(r,d)
is denoted as
\mathcal{H}_{G,r,d}
. When
G=\mathrm{GL}(r,\mathbb{C})
, the moduli space of
\mathrm{GL}
-Higgs pairs
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{GL}}(r,d)
is isomorphic via the associated bundle construction to the moduli space
\mathcal{M}(r,d)
of classical Higgs pairs of rank
r
and degree
d
.

Remark 1.3.11
.

For classical groups like
\mathrm{SL},\mathrm{Sp},\mathrm{SO}
, the basis
p_{1},\dots,p_{k}
can be chosen such that there is a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{G}(r,d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}(r,d)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}_{G}(r)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}(r).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}_{G,r,d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}_{r,d}}

Finally, the notion of stability can be defined for
G
-Higgs pairs, such that it is compatible with the notion of stability for the associated Higgs pairs when
G\subseteq\mathrm{GL}(r,\mathbb{C})
(see
[
Sch13
, Definition 2.4]
for details). Then, we have the following:

Definition 1.3.12
.

Let
G
be a complex reductive Lie group, let
\delta\in\pi_{1}(G)
and denote with
p_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,k
a homogeneous basis for the algebra of invariant polynomials of the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
of
G
, with degrees
d_{i}
.
The good moduli space
M_{G}(\delta)
of S-equivalence classes of semistable
G
-Higgs pairs
(P,\phi)
such that
P
has topological type
\delta
is endowed with a flat projective morphism, called the
G
-
Hitchin fibration
:

\displaystyle H_{G,\delta}^{p_{1},\dots,p_{k}}=H_{G,\delta}:M_{G}(\delta)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathcal{A}_{G}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}H^{0}(C,L^{d_{i}})

\displaystyle(P,\phi)

\displaystyle\mapsto(p_{1}(\phi),\dots,p_{k}(\phi)).

1.4.
The spectral correspondence for Higgs pairs

In this section, following
[
MRV17b
]
,
[
CL16
]
and
[
dC17
]
, we resume known facts about the spectral correspondence, which describes any fiber of the Hitchin morphism
\mathcal{H}
in terms of torsion-free rank-1 sheaves on an associated spectral curve.

Let
{\underline{a}}\in\mathcal{A}(r)
be any characteristic. The spectral curve
X_{\underline{a}}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\underline{a}}}C
is the projective scheme defined in the total space of
L
,
P=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{O}_{C}\oplus L^{-1})\xrightarrow{p}C
, by the homogeneous equation

x^{r}+p^{*}(a_{1})x^{r-1}y+\dots+p^{*}(a_{r})y^{r}=0

where
x
is the section of
\mathcal{O}_{P}(1)\otimes p^{*}(L)
whose pushforward via
p
corresponds to the constant section
(0,1)
of
L\oplus\mathcal{O}_{C}
and
y
is the section of
\mathcal{O}_{P}(1)
whose pushforward via
p
corresponds to the constant section
(1,0)
of
\mathcal{O}_{C}\oplus L^{-1}
. Note that the restriction of
y
to
X_{\underline{a}}
is everywhere non-zero and hence the restriction of
\mathcal{O}_{P}(1)
to
X_{\underline{a}}
is trivial. Moreover, the restriction of
x
to
X_{\underline{a}}
can be considered as a section of
\pi_{\underline{a}}^{*}(L)
.

We can now compute the canonical sheaf of
X_{\underline{a}}
.

Lemma 1.4.1
.

(Canonical sheaf of the spectral curve)

The canonical sheaf of
X_{\underline{a}}
is equal to

\omega_{X_{\underline{a}}}=\pi_{\underline{a}}^{*}(\omega_{c}\otimes L^{r-1}).

Proof.

By
[
Har77
, Chap. V, Lemma 2.10]
the canonical sheaf of
P
is equal to

\omega_{P}=\mathcal{O}_{P}(-2)\otimes p^{*}(\omega_{C}\otimes L^{-1})

The canonical sheaf of
X_{\underline{a}}
can be computed with the adjunction formula

\omega_{X_{\underline{a}}}=\left(\omega_{P}(X_{\underline{a}})\right)_{|X_{\underline{a}}}=\left(\mathcal{O}_{P}(r-2)+p^{*}(\omega_{c}\otimes L^{r-1})\right)_{|X_{\underline{a}}}.

Since
\mathcal{O}_{P}(1)_{|X_{\underline{a}}}
is trivial, we conclude that

\omega_{X_{\underline{a}}}=\pi_{\underline{a}}^{*}(\omega_{c}\otimes L^{r-1}).

∎

Corollary 1.4.2
.

Denote with
g
the genus of
C
and with
\ell
the degree of
L
on
C
. Then

\chi(X_{\underline{a}})=r(1-g)-\frac{r(r-1)}{2}\ell.

Proof.

The pullback of a line bundle by a finite map has degree equal to the degree of the bundle times the degree of the morphism. Then

\deg(\omega_{X_{\underline{a}}})=r\deg(\omega_{C})+r(r-1)\deg(L)=2r(g-1)+r(r-1)\ell.

The statement follows then by the fact that
\chi(X_{\underline{a}})=-\deg(\omega_{X_{\underline{a}}})/2
.
∎

Remark 1.4.3
.

The spectral curve
X_{\underline{a}}
has pure dimension 1 and is embedded in the smooth surface
P
