P=\operatorname{Fr}_{\mathrm{GSp}}(E,\omega)
of all ordered symplectic basis of
(E,\omega)
is a principal
\mathrm{GSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-bundle, and the Higgs field
\Phi
is the image of a unique global section
\phi
of
\ad P\otimes L
with respect to the morphism
\ad P\otimes L\rightarrow\operatorname{End}E\otimes L
induced by the canonical embedding
\rho:\mathrm{Sp}(2r,\mathbb{C})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{GL}(2r,\mathbb{C})
.

Tu sum up, the datum of a
\mathrm{GSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pair
(P,\phi)
corresponds uniquely, via the associated bundle construction, to the datum
(E,\Phi,M,\omega)
of a Higgs pair
(E,\Phi)
of rank
2r
and degree
rd
and a non-degenerate symplectic form
\omega:E\otimes E\rightarrow M
with values in a line bundle
M
of degree
d
, and
\Phi\in H^{0}(C,\operatorname{End}(E)\otimes L)
satisfies

(4.5)

\displaystyle\omega(\Phi v,w)+\omega(v,\Phi w)=\frac{\operatorname{tr}\Phi}{r}\omega(v,w).

Since
\mathfrak{gsp}(2r,\mathbb{C})\subset\mathfrak{gl}(2r,\mathbb{C})
, the
\mathrm{GSp}
-Hitchin morphism can be defined with the help of the
\mathrm{GL}
-Hitchin morphism, as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GSp},2r,rd}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{GSp}}(2r,rd)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GSp}}(2r)\subseteq\bigoplus_{i=1}^{2r}H^{0}(C,L^{i})

\displaystyle(P,\phi)

\displaystyle\longmapsto(p_{1}(P,\phi),\dots,p_{2r}(P,\phi)).

where
p_{i}(P,\phi)=(-)^{i}\operatorname{tr}(\wedge^{i}\phi)
and
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GSp}}(2r)
is defined as the locus of characteristics in
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GL}}(2r)
resulting as characteristics of
\mathrm{GSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pairs.

In order to study the space of characteristics
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GSp}}(2r)
, let
(E,\Phi)
be the Higgs pair associated to any
\mathrm{GSp}
-Higgs pair
(P,\phi)
and consider then the Higgs field

\Phi^{\prime}=\Phi-\frac{\operatorname{tr}\Phi}{2r}\id_{E}\in H^{0}(C,\operatorname{End}(E)\otimes L).

Reformulating Equation
4.5
, the following condition on
\Phi^{\prime}
holds:

(4.6)

\displaystyle\omega(\Phi^{\prime}v,w)+\omega(v,\Phi^{\prime}w)=0.

As in the case of
\mathrm{Sp}
-Higgs pairs, this condition implies that
a_{i}(E,\Phi^{\prime})=0
when
i=2l+1
. In particular, the vector
{\underline{a}}^{\prime}=(a_{2}(E,\Phi^{\prime}),a_{4}(E,\Phi^{\prime}),\dots,a_{2r}(E,\Phi^{\prime}))
may assume any value in the affine space

\bigoplus_{l=1}^{r}H^{0}(C,L^{2l})=\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(2r)\subset\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GL}}(2r)=\mathcal{A}(2r).

Let
{\underline{a}}=(a_{1}(E,\Phi),a_{2}(E,\Phi),\dots,a_{2r}(E,\Phi))\in\mathcal{A}(2r)
be the characteristic of
(E,\Phi)
. Since
a_{i}(E,\Phi)=p_{i}(P,\phi)
for any
i=0,\dots,2r
, we have that
{\underline{a}}
is equal to the characteristic of
(P,\phi)
in
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GSp}}(2r)
. What is the relation between
{\underline{a}}
and
{\underline{a}}^{\prime}
? Denote with
\chi_{\Phi}
and
\chi_{\Phi^{\prime}}
respectively the characteristic polynomials of
\Phi
and
\Phi^{\prime}
, and set
\frac{\operatorname{tr}\Phi}{2r}=\mu\in H^{0}(C,L)
. By definition of
\Phi^{\prime}
, the characteristic polynomials
\chi_{\Phi}
and
\chi_{\Phi^{\prime}}
are related by the following equality:

(4.7)

\displaystyle\chi_{\Phi}(t+\mu)=\det(\Phi-(t+\mu)\id_{E})=\det(\Phi^{\prime}-t\id_{E})=\chi_{\Phi^{\prime}}(t).

Comparing the coefficients in Equation
4.7
and recalling that
\operatorname{tr}\Phi=a_{1}(E,\Phi)
, it follows that the vector
({\underline{a}}^{\prime},\mu)
can be determined by a polynomial combination
{\underline{P}}({\underline{a}})
of the entries of
{\underline{a}}
. Viceversa, by the equality
\chi_{\Phi}(t)=\chi_{\Phi^{\prime}}(t-\mu)
, it follows that the vector
{\underline{a}}
can be determined back by a polynomial combination of the entries of the vector
{\underline{a}}^{\prime}
and the scalar
\mu
, denoted by
{\underline{Q}}({\underline{a}}^{\prime},\mu)
. In other words,
{\underline{P}}
defines an isomorphism between
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{GSp}}(2r)
and the affine space
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(2r)\oplus H^{0}(C,L)
, with inverse
{\underline{Q}}
.

By means of the previous discussion, the datum of a
\mathrm{GSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pair
(P,\phi)
corresponds uniquely, via the associated bundle construction and the translation of the Higgs field, to the datum
(E,\Phi^{\prime},\omega,M,\mu)
of a Higgs pair
(E,\Phi^{\prime})
of rank
2r
and degree
rd
, a non-degenerate symplectic form
\omega:E\otimes E\rightarrow M
with values in a line bundle
M
of degree
d
, and a global global section
\mu\in H^{0}(C,L)
, such that
\Phi^{\prime}\in H^{0}(C,\operatorname{End}(E)\otimes L)
satisfies

(4.8)

\displaystyle\omega(\Phi^{\prime}v,w)+\omega(v,\Phi^{\prime}w)=0.

Moreover, the affine space
\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(2r)\oplus H^{0}(C,L)
can be taken as basis of another
\mathrm{GSp}
-Hitchin morphism
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}={\underline{P}}\circ\mathcal{H}
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{GSp},2r,rd}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{GSp}}(2r,rd)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(2r)\oplus H^{0}(C,L)

\displaystyle(P,\phi)

\displaystyle\longmapsto({\underline{a}}^{\prime},\mu)={\underline{P}}(p_{1}(P,\phi),\dots,p_{2r}(P,\phi))=

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt=(a_{2}(E,\Phi^{\prime}),a_{4}(E,\Phi^{\prime}),\dots,a_{2r}(E,\Phi^{\prime}),\frac{\operatorname{tr}\Phi}{2r}).

Proposition 4.4.1
.

(Spectral correspondence for
\mathrm{GSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
)

Let
{\underline{a}}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{Sp}}(2r)
be any characteristic and let
\mu\in H^{0}(C,L)
be any section. Let
X=X_{{\underline{a}}^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\pi}C

be the spectral curve associated to
{\underline{a}}^{\prime}
and let
\sigma
be the involution defined on
X
as in Section
4.3
.
Let
d^{\prime}=rd+r(2r-1)\ell
and denote with
\mathcal{P}(d^{\prime},n)=\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d^{\prime})\times J^{d}(C)
the Cartesian product of the compactified Jacobian of degree
d^{\prime}
on
X
and the Jacobian of degree
d
on
C
, endowed with the projection maps
p_{X}
and
p_{C}
on
\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d^{\prime})
and
J^{d}(C)
respectively. Let
\mathcal{E}_{{\underline{a}}^{\prime}}
be the equalizer of the two maps

\displaystyle(\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\texttt{\char 95\relax},\mathcal{O}_{X})\circ p_{X})\otimes(\pi^{*}\circ p_{C}):\mathcal{P}(d^{\prime},d)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,rd-r(2r-1)\ell)

\displaystyle(\sigma^{*}\circ p_{X})\otimes\pi^{*}L^{1-2r}:\mathcal{P}(d^{\prime},d)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,rd-r(2r-1)\ell).

Then, the fiber
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{GSp},2r,rd}^{-1}({\underline{a}}^{\prime},\mu)
is isomorphic, via the spectral correspondence, to
\mathcal{E}_{{\underline{a}}^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

The datum of a
\mathrm{GSp}
-Higgs pair
(P,\phi)
in the fiber
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{GSp},2r,rd}^{-1}({\underline{a}}^{\prime},\mu)
corresponds uniquely to the datum
(E,\Phi^{\prime},\omega,M,\mu)
of a Higgs pair
(E,\Phi^{\prime})
with rank
2r
and characteristic
{\underline{a}}^{\prime}
, a non-degenerate symplectic form
\omega
on
E
with values in a line bundle
M
of degree
d
on
C
, and
\mu\in H^{0}(C,L)
fixed. The torsion-free sheaf
\mathcal{M}
on
X
corresponding to
(E,\Phi^{\prime})
by the spectral correspondence fits into the exact sequence:

0\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}L^{1-2r}\rightarrow\pi^{*}E\xrightarrow{\pi^{*}\Phi-x}\pi^{*}(E\otimes L)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}L\rightarrow 0.

Taking the dualized sequence and tensoring by
\pi^{*}(L\otimes M)
gives a left exact sequence:

0\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}\otimes M\rightarrow\pi^{*}(E^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}\otimes M)\xrightarrow{\pi^{*}\Phi^{t}-x}\pi^{*}(E^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}\otimes M)\otimes\pi^{*}L.

On the other hand, applying
\sigma^{*}
to the first exact sequence gives:

0\rightarrow\sigma^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}L^{1-2r}\rightarrow\pi^{*}E\xrightarrow{\pi^{*}\Phi+x}\pi^{*}(E\otimes L)\rightarrow\sigma^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}L\rightarrow 0.

Now, the non-degenerate symplectic form
\omega
induces an isomorphism
\omega_{E}:E\rightarrow E^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}\otimes M
and hence a commutative diagram with vertical isomorphisms:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}\otimes M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{*}(E^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}\otimes M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{*}(E^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}\otimes M)\otimes\pi^{*}L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}L^{1-2r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{*}E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{*}(E\otimes L)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{*}\Phi^{t}-x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\pi^{*}\Phi-x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Hence, we conclude that the sheaf
\mathcal{M}
comes with sheaf isomorphism

\lambda:\mathcal{M}^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}\otimes\pi^{*}M\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\sigma^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}L^{1-2r}.

Viceversa, suppose that
\mathcal{M}
is a torsion-free sheaf of rank 1 and degree
d^{\prime}
on
X
with a line bundle
M
of degree
d
on
C
and an isomorphism
\lambda:\mathcal{M}^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}\otimes\pi^{*}M\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\sigma^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}L^{1-2r}
. Let
(E,\Phi^{\prime})
be the Higgs pairs of rank
2r
and degree
rd
corresponding to
\mathcal{M}
by the spectral correspondence. Applying Relative duality in dimension 0 and the projection formula as in Section
4.3
, we have an isomorphism:

\displaystyle E^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}

\displaystyle=\pi_{*}(\mathcal{M})^{*}\simeq\pi_{*}(\mathcal{M}^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}\otimes\pi^{*}L^{2r-1})\simeq

\displaystyle\simeq\pi_{*}(\sigma^{*}(\mathcal{M})\otimes\pi^{*}M^{-1})\simeq

\displaystyle\simeq\pi_{*}(\sigma^{*}(\mathcal{M}))\otimes M^{-1}=\pi_{*}(\sigma_{*}\sigma^{*}\mathcal{M})\otimes M^{-1}\simeq

\displaystyle\simeq\pi_{*}(\sigma_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}\otimes\mathcal{M})\otimes M^{-1}=\pi_{*}(\mathcal{O}_{X}\otimes\mathcal{M})\otimes M^{-1}\simeq

\displaystyle\simeq\pi_{*}(\mathcal{M})\otimes M^{-1}=E\otimes M^{-1};

that induces the non-degenerate
M
-valued symplectic structure
\omega:E\otimes E\rightarrow M
.

To sum up, the datum of
(P,\phi)
in the fiber
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GSp},2r,rd}^{-1}({\underline{a}})
corresponds uniquely to the datum
(\mathcal{M},M,\lambda)
of a torsion-free rank-1 sheaf
\mathcal{M}\in\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d^{\prime})
, a line bundle
M\in J(C,d)
and an isomorphism
\lambda:\mathcal{M}^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}\otimes\pi^{*}M\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\sigma^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}L^{1-2r}
, i.e. an element of the equalizer stack
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}
.
∎

4.5.
\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pairs

The Projective Symplectic group
\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
is defined by the exact sequence:

(4.9)

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{*}\xrightarrow{\lambda\mapsto\lambda I_{2r}}\mathrm{GSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})\rightarrow\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})\rightarrow 0

or by the exact sequence:

(4.10)

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\{\pm 1\}\xrightarrow{1\mapsto I_{2r}}\mathrm{Sp}(2r,\mathbb{C})\rightarrow\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})\rightarrow 0.

In particular, the sheaf-theoretic version of sequence
4.9
yields the cohomology exact sequence:

(4.11)

\displaystyle H^{1}(C,\mathcal{O}_{C}^{*})\rightarrow H^{1}(C,\mathrm{GSp}(2r,\mathcal{O}_{C})){\mathrel{\to}^{q}}H^{1}(C,\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathcal{O}_{C}))\rightarrow 0.

This surjection, read in terms of cocycles, means that
\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-principal bundles are in one-to-one correspondence with equivalence classes of
\mathrm{GSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-principal bundles, with respect to the action on associated bundles given by tensor product of line bundles. If
P
is a
\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-principal bundle and
\tilde{P}
is a
\mathrm{GSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-principal bundle such that
q(\tilde{P)}=P
, we say that
\tilde{P}
is a lifting of
P
to a
\mathrm{GSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-principal bundle.

Moreover, by sequence
4.10
,
\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
is the quotient of
\mathrm{Sp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
by the action of a finite group, hence the associated Lie algebras
\mathfrak{psp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
and
\mathfrak{sp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
are equal. If
P
is any
\mathrm{PSp}(r2,\mathbb{C})
-principal bundle and
\tilde{P}
is a lifting of
P
to a
\mathrm{GSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-principal bundle, then a section
\phi
of
H^{0}(C,\ad P\otimes L)
determines uniquely a section
\tilde{\phi}
of
H^{0}(C,\ad\tilde{P}\otimes L)
with trace equal to 0, and viceversa. We say that
(\tilde{P},\tilde{\phi})
is a lifting of
(P,\phi)
to a
\mathrm{GSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pair and with a slight abuse of notation we write
q(\tilde{P},\tilde{\phi})=(P,\phi)
.

To sum up, any
\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pair
(P,\phi)
has a lifting
(\tilde{P},\tilde{\phi})
to a
\mathrm{GSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pair with trace zero, corresponding via the associated bundle construction to the datum
(E,\Phi,M,\omega)
of a Higgs pair
(E,\Phi)
of rank
2r
, a non-degenerate symplectic form
\omega:E\otimes E\rightarrow M
with values in a line bundle
M
of degree
d
on
C
, and a Higgs field
\Phi
satisfying the condition

\omega(\Phi v,w)+\omega(v,\Phi w)=0.

Then, the datum of
(P,\phi)
corresponds uniquely to the datum of the equivalence class
[(E,\Phi,M,\omega)]
of Higgs pairs of rank
2r
with a non-degenerate symplectic form, under the equivalence relation
\sim_{J(C)}
defined by:

(E,\Phi,M\omega)\sim_{J(C)}(E\otimes N,\Phi\otimes 1_{N},M\otimes N^{2},\omega_{N})\hskip 20.00003pt\textrm{for any }N\in J(C)

where

\omega_{N}:(E\otimes N)\otimes(E\otimes N)\rightarrow M\otimes N^{2}

is obtained by extension of scalars.

More precisely, let
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{GSp}}(2r)=\coprod_{d\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{GSp}}(2r,rd)
be the moduli stack of
\mathrm{GSp}
-Higgs pairs of rank
2r
in any degree and denote with
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{GSp}}^{\operatorname{tr}=0}(2r)
the closed substack of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{GSp}}(2r)
given by
\mathrm{GSp}
-Higgs pairs of rank
2r
with trace zero. Then,
[(E,\Phi,M,\omega)]
is the orbit of
(E,\Phi,M,\omega)
under the action of
J(C)
on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{GSp}}^{\operatorname{tr}=0}(2r)
defined by:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{GSp}}^{\operatorname{tr}=0}(2r)\times J(C)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{GSp}}^{\operatorname{tr}=0}(2r)

\displaystyle((E,\Phi,M,\omega),N)

\displaystyle\longmapsto(E\otimes N,\Phi\otimes 1_{N},M\otimes N^{2},\omega_{N}).

Recall that the isomorphism
\det E\simeq M^{r}
implies that
\deg(E)=r\deg(M)
; in other words, the degree of the Higgs pair in the datum
(E,\Phi,M,\omega)
is determined by the degree of the line bundle
M
in the same datum. The action of
J(C)
on such datum modifies the degree of
M
by multiples of
2
, since
\deg(M\otimes N^{2})=\deg(M)+2\deg(N)
. This his fact implies that the degree of
\mathrm{GSp}
-Higgs pairs associated to a
\mathrm{PSp}
-Higgs pair is defined only modulo
2r
. Then, we can give the following definition.

Definition 4.5.1
.

Let
(P,\phi)
be a
\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pair and let
(E,\Phi,M,\omega)
be the datum of a Higgs pair with trace zero and degree
rd
endowed with a
M
-valued symplectic form
\omega
, corresponding to a lifting
(\tilde{P},\tilde{\phi})
of
(P,\phi)
to a
\mathrm{GSp}
-Higgs pair. The
degree of

(P,\phi)
is the congruence class
\overline{rd}\in\mathbb{Z}/2r\mathbb{Z}
.

Remark 4.5.2
.

The same definition works for
\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-principal bundles, without Higgs pair. The degree of a
\mathrm{PSp}
-principal bundle
P
is the congruence class (modulo
2r
) of the degree of the vector bundles associated to any lifting
\tilde{P}
of
P
to a
\mathrm{GSp}
-principal bundle.

Clearly, only two cases are possible: the degree of a
\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pair
(P,\phi)
is congruent either to
0
or to
r
modulo
2r
. This reflects the fact that the topological type of
P
is parametrized by
\pi_{1}(\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C}))\simeq\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
.

Up to the action on
(E,\Phi,M,\omega)
of a line bundle of degree
1
on
C
, it is straightforward to see that the
J(C)
-orbit
[(E,\Phi,M,\omega)]
corresponding to a
\mathrm{PGL}
-Higgs pair with degree
\overline{rd}\in\mathbb{Z}/2r\mathbb{Z}
is always the orbit of a datum whose Higgs pair has degree
0
or
rd
. If we restrict the correspondence to Higgs pairs with fixed degree
0
or
rd
, we see that a
\mathrm{PGL}
-Higgs pair of degree
\overline{rd}
is identified uniquely by the orbit of a Higgs pair with trace zero
and degree
0
or
rd
with respect to the action of line bundles
of degree

0
on
C
.

A basis for the invariant polynomials of
\mathfrak{psp}(2r,\mathbb{C})=\mathfrak{sp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
is given by
\{p_{2i}\}_{i=1,\dots,r}
where
p_{2i}(P,\phi):=\operatorname{tr}(\wedge^{2i}\phi)
. The corresponding
\mathrm{PSp}
-Hitchin morphism takes the form:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{PSp},2r,\overline{rd}}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{PSp}}(2r,\overline{rd})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{PSp}}(2r)=\bigoplus_{l=1}^{r}H^{0}(C,L^{2l})

\displaystyle(P,\phi)

\displaystyle\longmapsto(p_{2}(P,\phi),p_{4}(P,\phi),\dots,p_{2r}(P,\phi)).

If
(\tilde{P},\tilde{\phi})
is any lifting of
(P,\phi)
to a
\mathrm{GSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pair, then
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{GSp},2r,rd}(\tilde{P},\tilde{\phi})=\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{PSp},2r,\overline{rd}}(P,\phi)
. Moreover, for any characteristic
{\underline{a}}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{PSp}}(r)
, the spectral curve
\pi:X_{\underline{a}}\rightarrow C
is defined in the total space
p:\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{O}_{C}\oplus L^{-1})\rightarrow C
by the equation

x^{2r}y+a_{2}x^{2r-2}y^{2}+\dots+a_{2r-2}x^{2}y^{2r-2}+a_{2r}y^{2r}=0.

Hence, as in the case of
\mathrm{Sp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pairs, the curve
X_{\underline{a}}
has an involution defined by
\sigma(x)=-x
.

We can now state the following proposition.

Proposition 4.5.3
.

(Spectral correspondence for
\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
)

Let
{\underline{a}}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{PSp}}(2r)
be any characteristic, let
X=X_{\underline{a}}\xrightarrow{\pi}C
be the associated spectral curve with involution
\sigma:X\rightarrow X
. Let
d\in\{0,1\}
,
d^{\prime}=rd+r(2r-1)\ell
and denote with
\mathcal{P}(d^{\prime},n)=\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d^{\prime})\times J^{d}(C)
the Cartesian product of the compactified Jacobian of degree
d^{\prime}
on
X
and the Jacobian of degree
d
on
C
, endowed with the projection maps
p_{X}
and
p_{C}
on
\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d^{\prime})
and
J^{d}(C)
respectively. Let
\mathcal{E}_{{\underline{a}}^{\prime}}
be the equalizer of the two maps

\displaystyle(\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\texttt{\char 95\relax},\mathcal{O}_{X})\circ p_{X})\otimes(\pi^{*}\circ p_{C}):\mathcal{P}(d^{\prime},d)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,rd-r(2r-1)\ell)

\displaystyle(\sigma^{*}\circ p_{X})\otimes\pi^{*}L^{1-2r}:\mathcal{P}(d^{\prime},d)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,rd-r(2r-1)\ell).

The group
J^{0}(C)
of line bundles of degree
0
on
C
acts on
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}\times J^{0}(C)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}

\displaystyle((\mathcal{M},M,\lambda),N)

\displaystyle\longmapsto(\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}N,M\otimes N^{2},\lambda_{N})

where
\lambda_{N}
is given by the composition of
\lambda\otimes\id_{\pi^{*}N}
with the canonical isomorphisms:

\displaystyle(\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}N)^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}\otimes\pi^{*}(M\otimes N^{2})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathcal{M}^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}\otimes(\pi^{*}N)^{-1}\otimes\pi^{*}M\otimes(\pi^{*}N)^{2}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathcal{M}^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}\otimes\pi^{*}M\otimes\pi^{*}N\xrightarrow{\lambda\otimes\id_{\pi^{*}N}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\sigma^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}L^{1-2r}\otimes\pi^{*}N\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\sigma^{*}(\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}N)\otimes\pi^{*}L^{1-2r}.

Then, the fiber
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{PSp},2r,\overline{rd}}^{-1}({\underline{a}})
of the
\mathrm{PSp}
-Hitchin morphism is isomorphic, via the spectral correspondence, to the quotient
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}/J^{0}(C)
.

Proof.

Let
(P,\phi)
be any
\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pair with characteristic
{\underline{a}}
and let
(E,\Phi,M,\omega)
be the datum of a Higgs pair with a non-degenerate
M
-valued symplectic form such that
(P\phi)
corresponds uniquely to the datum of the
J^{0}(C)
-equivalence class
[(E,\Phi,M,\omega)]
.

By Proposition
4.4.1
, the datum of
(E,\Phi,M,\omega)
corresponds, via the spectral correspondence, to the datum
(\mathcal{M},M,\lambda)
of a torsion-free rank-1 sheaf
\mathcal{M}\in\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d^{\prime})
, the line bundle
M\in J^{d}(C)
and an isomorphism
\lambda:\mathcal{M}^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}\otimes\pi^{*}M\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\sigma^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}L^{1-2r}
, i.e. an element of
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}
. If
N
is any line bundle of degree
0
on
C
, then by the projection formula the datum of
(E\otimes N,\Phi\otimes 1_{N},M\otimes N^{2},\omega_{N})
corresponds to the datum of
(\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}N,M\otimes N^{2},\lambda_{N})
.

We conclude that the datum of the
J^{0}(C)
-orbit
[(E,\Phi,M,\omega)]
corresponds, via the spectral correspondence, to the datum of the
J^{0}(C)
-orbit of
(\mathcal{M},M,\lambda)
.
∎

A particular case occurs for
d=0
, i.e. for
\mathrm{PGL}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pairs of degree
0
. Indeed, recall the sequence
4.9
:

0\rightarrow\{\pm 1\}\xrightarrow{1\mapsto I_{2r}}\mathrm{Sp}(2r,\mathbb{C})\rightarrow\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})\rightarrow 0.

Recalla also that
\{\pm 1\}=\mu_{2}
and
H^{2}(C,\mu_{2})=\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
. Hence this sequence, applied to structure sheaves, induces the cohomology exact sequence:

H^{1}(C,\mu_{2})\rightarrow H^{1}(C,\mathrm{Sp}(2r,\mathcal{O}_{C})){\mathrel{\to}^{q}}H^{1}(C,\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathcal{O}_{C}))\xrightarrow{\deg/r}\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow 0

where the last map sends a
\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-principal bundle of degree
\overline{rd}
to the congruence class
\overline{d}
. This sequence, read in terms of cocycles, means that a
\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-principal bundle
P
can be lifted to
\mathrm{Sp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-principal bundle
P_{0}
if and only
P
has degree equal to
0\in\mathbb{Z}/2r\mathbb{Z}
; any other lifting
P_{0}^{\prime}
of
P
differs from
P_{0}
by the action on the associated bundles of a
2
-torsion line bundle by tensor product. Moreover, a Higgs field
\phi
on
P
determines uniquely a a Higgs field
\phi_{0}
on
P
and viceversa.

To sum up, any
\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pair
(P,\phi)
of degree
0
has a lifting
(\tilde{P},\tilde{\phi})
to a
\mathrm{Sp}(r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pair, corresponding to the datum
(E,\Phi,\omega)
of a Higgs pair of rank
2r
and degree
0
and a non-degenerate symplectic form
\omega
via the associated bundle construction. Then, the datum of
(P,\phi)
corresponds uniquely to the datum of the equivalence class
[(E,\Phi,\omega)]
of Higgs pairs of rank
2r
(and degree
0
) with a non-degenerate symplectic form, under the equivalence relation
\sim_{J^{0}(C)[2]}
defined by:

(E,\Phi,\omega)\sim_{J^{0}(C)[2]}(E\otimes N,\Phi\otimes 1_{N},\omega_{N,\epsilon})\hskip 20.00003pt\textrm{for any }(N,\epsilon)\in J^{0}(C)[2].

Here,
J^{0}(C)[2]
denotes the group stack parametrizing line bundles with
2
-torsion
(N,\epsilon)
on
C
, where
\epsilon
is the isomorphism
N^{-1}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}N
, and
\omega_{N,\epsilon}
is obtained by extension of scalars, as follows:

\omega_{N,\epsilon}:(E\otimes N)\otimes(E\otimes N)\rightarrow N^{2}\xrightarrow[\epsilon^{-1}\otimes 1]{{}_{\sim}}N^{-1}\otimes N\xrightarrow[\ev]{{}_{\sim}}\mathcal{O}_{C}.

We can finally state the following proposition.

Proposition 4.5.4
.

(Spectral correspondence for
\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
of degree
0
)

Let
{\underline{a}}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{PSp}}(2r)
be any characteristic and let
X=X_{\underline{a}}\xrightarrow{\pi}C
be the associated spectral curve with involution
\sigma:X\rightarrow X
. Let
d^{\prime}=r(2r-1)\ell
and let
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}
be the equalizer stack of the two maps:

\displaystyle\texttt{\char 95\relax}^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}:=\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\texttt{\char 95\relax},\mathcal{O}_{X}):\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d^{\prime})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,-d^{\prime})

\displaystyle\sigma^{*}\texttt{\char 95\relax}\otimes\pi^{*}L^{1-2r}:\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d^{\prime})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,-d^{\prime})

The group stack
J^{0}(C)[2]
of 2-torsion line bundles on
C
acts on
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}\times\pi^{*}J^{0}(C)[2]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}

\displaystyle((\mathcal{M},\lambda),(\pi^{*}N,\pi^{*}\epsilon))

\displaystyle\longmapsto(\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}N,\lambda\otimes\pi^{*}\epsilon).

Then, the fiber
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{PSp},2r,\overline{0}}^{-1}({\underline{a}})
of the
\mathrm{PSp}
-Hitchin morphism is isomorphic, via the spectral correspondence, to the quotient
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}/\pi^{*}J^{0}(C)[2]
.

Proof.

Let
(P,\phi)
be any
\mathrm{PSp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pair with degree
0
and characteristic
{\underline{a}}
and let
(\tilde{P},\tilde{\phi})
be any lifting to a
\mathrm{Sp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pair. By construction,
(\tilde{P},\tilde{\phi})
is a
\mathrm{Sp}(2r,\mathbb{C})
-Higgs pair with characteristic
{\underline{a}}
and corresponds uniquely, via the associated bundle construction, to the datum
(E,\Phi,\omega)
of a Higgs pair of rank
2r
and degree
0
with characteristic
{\underline{a}}
and a non-degenerate symplectic form. Then, the datum of
(P,\phi)
corresponds uniquely to the datum of the
J^{0}(C)[2]
-equivalence class
[(E,\Phi,\omega)]
.

By Proposition
4.3.1
, the datum of
(E,\Phi,\omega)
corresponds to the datum
(\mathcal{M},\lambda)
of a torsion-free rank-1 sheaf
\mathcal{M}\in\overline{\mathbb{J}}(X,d^{\prime})
and an isomorphism
\lambda:\mathcal{M}^{\smash{\scalebox{.7}[1.4]{\rotatebox{90.0}{{\T1\guilsinglleft}}}}}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\sigma^{*}\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}L^{1-2r}
, i.e. an element of
\mathcal{E}_{\underline{a}}
. If
N
is any
2
-torsion line bundle on
C
with isomorphism
\epsilon:N^{-1}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}N
, then by the projection formula the datum of
(E\otimes N,\Phi\otimes 1_{N},\omega_{N})
corresponds to the datum of
(\mathcal{M}\otimes\pi^{*}N,\lambda\otimes\pi^{*}\epsilon)
.

We conclude that the datum of the
J^{0}(C)[2]
-orbit
[(E,\Phi,\omega)]
corresponds to the datum of the
\pi^{*}J^{0}(C)[2]
-orbit of
(\mathcal{M},\lambda)
.
∎
